### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Coze Studio is licensed Apache-2.0 (OSI approved), self-hosted commercial internal use permitted with nothing behind a paid tier; Restrictions: None; LICENSE-APACHE is verbatim Apache-2.0 with no added clauses; every Go header carries the Apache notice; no ee/enterprise/pro dir, no licence-key check, no feature flag reading a tier; every backend "enterprise" grep hit is a display enum, a permission constant, or an icon URI; the only gap is that ListRootSpans / GetTraceSDK are compiled-in but stubbed; two non-license constraints are the README §Security public-network warning and Apache §6 trademarks; coze-loop is also verbatim Apache-2.0 with the APPENDIX retained.

CORRECTED: The licensing core of the claim is correct, but two of its supporting assertions are wrong, and one of them ("no feature flag reading a tier", "nothing behind a paid tier", "Restrictions: None") is materially overstated.

VERIFIED AS STATED:
- Repo root has LICENSE-APACHE and no file named LICENSE (root listing of coze-dev/coze-studio). README line 97 links the label "[LICENSE]" at .../blob/main/LICENSE-APACHE, and the badge at line 10 reads "license-apache2.0".
- LICENSE-APACHE is verbatim Apache License 2.0, terminating at "END OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS" with the APPENDIX omitted. No Commons Clause, no non-compete, no user-count cap, no trademark clause beyond §6, no service/hosting rider. Apache-2.0 is OSI-approved, so self-hosted internal commercial use is permitted.
- coze-dev/coze-loop LICENSE is verbatim Apache-2.0 and does retain the APPENDIX.
- Per-file notices agree with repo-level licensing, and this is CI-enforced, not merely observed: .github/workflows/license-check.yaml plus .github/.licenserc.yaml, with scripts/hooks/add-license-header.sh. A search for "MIT License" in Go files returns zero hits.
- The stub claim is exactly right, including the line numbers. backend/api/handler/coze/workflow_service.go:630 ListRootSpans and :646 GetTraceSDK each BindAndValidate the request, then `resp := new(...Response)` and `c.JSON(consts.StatusOK, resp)` with no service call. Both are unconditionally routed (backend/api/router/coze/api.go: POST /api/workflow_api/list_spans and /api/workflow_api/get_trace) and their middleware hooks are empty (`_listrootspansMw` / `_gettracesdkMw` in backend/api/router/coze/middleware.go return nil with a "// your code..." placeholder). The frontend calls both for real (frontend/packages/workflow/test-run-next/trace/src/components/trace-select/use-options.ts, .../trace-graph/use-trace.ts), so the trace UI is wired to endpoints that return an empty body with HTTP 200. Not gated — absent for everyone, and it looks wired up.

WRONG AS STATED (1) — the "enterprise" grep is not exhaustive, and a tier read does exist. Beyond the display enum and permission constant the claim lists, the OSS Go backend contains a live entitlement client against the hosted Coze SaaS:
- backend/domain/user/entity/saas_user.go defines BenefitType ("call_tool_limit", "api_run_qps"), UserLevel ("unknown"/"basic"/"v1_pro_instance"/"enterprise"), EntityBenefitStatus (valid/expired), ResourceUsageStrategy (quota/unlimit), and GetEnterpriseBenefitRequest/Response with quota counters (Used/Total/StartAt/EndAt).
- backend/domain/user/service/user_saas_impl.go: CozeUserService.GetEnterpriseBenefit HTTP-calls the SaaS API and populates userBenefit.UserLevel from the response; GetUserBenefit requests exactly "call_tool_limit,api_run_qps" for ResourceID "plugin". backend/domain/user/service/user.go declares the SaasUserProvider interface (GetSaasUserInfo, GetUserBenefit).
- backend/pkg/saasapi/client.go: BaseURL comes from PluginConfiguration.CozeSaasAPIBaseURL and DEFAULTS TO https://api.coze.cn, authenticated with PluginConfiguration.CozeAPIToken.
- backend/application/plugin/plugin.go GetProductCallInfo consumes it, and the enum mapping the claim cites at :608 sits inside that consumer, filling UserLevel plus ProductCallCountLimit (IsUnlimited/UsedCount/TotalCount/ResetDatetime) and ProductCallRateLimit (QPS).
So it is accurate that no source-code feature is licence-gated and there is no ee/, enterprise/ or pro/ directory and no licence-key check — but "no feature flag reading a tier" is false. A self-hosted instance's official-marketplace plugin call quota and QPS are read from your Coze SaaS account tier over the network; without a CozeAPIToken that path errors out rather than degrading. It is remote-quota-dependent, not paid-code-gated.

WRONG AS STATED (2) — the security warning is misattributed and slightly misquoted. It is not in README "§Security"; README's "## Security and privacy" (line 100) is only about reporting vulnerabilities to the ByteDance security center. The warning is a `> [!WARNING]` block at README line 71, inside the Quickstart install steps immediately after "Visit Coze Studio at http://localhost:8888/". Its exact text reads "...it is recommended to assess security risks before you begin, and take corresponding protection measures" (the claim drops the protection-measures clause) and "SSRF (Server - Side Request Forgery)" with spaces, and it links to the wiki Quickstart#security-risks-in-public-networks.

Also, "Restrictions offered: None" is not right even on the claim's own terms: Apache-2.0 §4 imposes redistribution conditions (license copy, change notices, retained notices, NOTICE propagation) and §6 withholds trademark rights, which the claim itself concedes later.

REASONING: I read the primary sources rather than badges. LICENSE-APACHE and coze-loop's LICENSE were read in full and both are verbatim Apache-2.0 with no riders, matching the claim including the APPENDIX-trimmed/APPENDIX-retained distinction; per-file headers are CI-enforced by .github/workflows/license-check.yaml. The two stubbed handlers were fetched raw and confirmed line-for-line at :630 and :646, including that their router middleware hooks are empty placeholders and the frontend trace components call them for real — so the claim's most damaging admission is correct and understated if anything. The refutation rests on the claim's own supporting evidence being incomplete: a case-insensitive "enterprise" search over the Go backend surfaces backend/domain/user/entity/saas_user.go and backend/domain/user/service/user_saas_impl.go, an entitlement client that HTTP-calls https://api.coze.cn (default) with an API token to fetch user_level and call_tool_limit / api_run_qps quotas, consumed by GetProductCallInfo in the very file the claim cites. That is a tier-and-quota read the claim explicitly denied exists, which changes the answer to "is anything behind a paid tier": marketplace plugin call limits in a self-hosted instance track your Coze SaaS account tier. Separately the README security quote is attributed to the wrong section and is missing a clause. Per the instruction that overstated counts as refuted, refuted=true.

### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Coze Studio allows tool definitions AND their implementations to be authored inside the builder rather than in our application source, via the workflow Code node (Python only, `async def main(args)`), persisted in the `canvas` column, executed server-side, bound to an agent as a callable tool, streaming via `MessageTypeToolMidAnswer`.

CORRECTED: Directionally right, wrong in three checkable specifics, and narrower than the requirement.

VERIFIED (Apache-2.0, all in the OSS repo, commit fefb05ff): the plugin split is exactly as stated — `tool_draft` stores `sub_url` varchar(512), `method` varchar(64), `operation` json ("Tool Openapi Operation Schema") and `plugin_draft` stores `server_url` + `openapi_doc`, so plugins are definition-only and execute as an outbound HTTP call to a server you run (helm/charts/opencoze/files/mysql/schema.sql). `NodeTypeCodeRunner` exists with DisplayKey "Code" and EnUS description "Write code to process input variables to generate return values" (backend/domain/workflow/entity/node_meta.go:332). `workflow_draft`/`workflow_version` both have `canvas` mediumtext "Front end schema". `Runner.Invoke` → `coderunner.GetCodeRunner().Run(...)` is real (backend/domain/workflow/internal/nodes/code/code.go). `single_agent_draft`/`single_agent_version` both have `workflow` json "Agent Workflow Configuration", and workflows really do become agent tools: `agentTools = append(agentTools, slices.Transform(wfTools, func(a workflow.ToolFromWorkflow) tool.BaseTool {...}))` and any tool at all forces `isReActAgent = true` (agent_flow_builder.go:135-165). The entrypoint contract is as stated — both runner wrapper templates define `class Args: self.params = params` and call `asyncio.run(main(Args(...)))`, expecting a dict (direct/runner.go `pythonCode`; script/sandbox.py `prefix`/`suffix`).

WRONG 1 — the streaming path. A Code node's output does NOT reach `MessageTypeToolMidAnswer`. `AgentRunner.processWfMidAnswerStream` drops every frame that is not an output node: `if msg.DataMessage.NodeType != crossworkflow.NodeTypeOutputEmitter { continue }` (agent_flow_runner.go:150-152). The code node's return value is the tool result the model then consumes and paraphrases; incremental mid-answer streaming requires adding an Output/message-emitter node to the workflow.

WRONG 2 — where Python-only is enforced. `convertCodeLanguage` DOES accept JavaScript: `case 5: return coderunner.JavaScript, nil` (code.go). The gate is one function later, in `Config.Build`: `if c.Language != coderunner.Python { return nil, errors.New("only support python language") }`. The sandbox runner's `fmt.Errorf("js not supported yet")` is a second, redundant gate. The conclusion (Python only) holds; the cited mechanism does not.

NARROWER 3 — and this is the part that defeats the original requirement. The DEFAULT runner is the sandbox, not the direct one: an unset `CODE_RUNNER_TYPE` resolves to `CodeRunnerType_Sandbox` (`runnerTypeStr == "sandbox" || runnerTypeStr == ""`, backend/bizpkg/config/base/base.go:68-69). That runner shells `python3 sandbox.py`, which shells `deno run … jsr:@langchain/pyodide-sandbox@0.0.4` — Pyodide/WASM, with permissions built only from `CODE_RUNNER_ALLOW_NET`/`_ENV`/`_READ`/`_WRITE`/`_RUN`/`_FFI`. None of those keys appear anywhere in `docker/.env.example` (grep count: 0), so `build_permission_flag` emits no `--allow-net` and Deno denies network. Out of the box the Code node can host pure computation only; any tool that must call a service (our case) still needs a plugin pointing at a server we host — i.e. the implementation stays in our TypeScript after all. Imports are additionally restricted to `pythonBuiltinModules` minus a blacklist (socket, threading, multiprocessing, pty…) plus a configured third-party whitelist (`validatePythonImports`).

Reliability caveat: issue #2548 (OPEN, last touched 2026-03-27) is a live defect in this exact path — sandbox.py builds the program by string interpolation, `code = prefix + f'args={json.dumps(params)}\n' + user_code + suffix`, so upstream input values blow up compilation with `SyntaxError: unterminated string literal`. #2585 (open) reports a Code node that runs standalone but errors inside the full workflow.

REASONING: Read a local full checkout of coze-dev/coze-studio at commit fefb05ff (/tmp/coze-probe/coze-studio) rather than docs. Licence is plain Apache-2.0 with no added terms, and every schema and Go symbol the claim names exists, so the core capability is not fabricated. Three things break it as stated: the ToolMidAnswer filter excludes non-output-emitter nodes, so the streaming assertion is false for a code node; the Python-only enforcement is in `Config.Build`, not `convertCodeLanguage`, which explicitly accepts JavaScript; and the default sandbox grants no network permission, which means the "real implementation authored in the UI" cannot reach any external service without extra env configuration — the precise thing the requirement was trying to achieve. Open issue #2548 confirms the param-marshalling in the default runner is broken for ordinary string inputs.

### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Coze Studio streams token-level model output incrementally during an agent turn that includes tool calls, via POST /v3/chat, POST /api/conversation/chat, and POST /v1/workflow/stream_run + /v1/workflows/chat; frames are WHATWG SSE from hertz-contrib/sse v0.1.0 (`event:<name>\ndata:<json>\n\n`, no id:/retry:), event names from agentrun/entity/const.go with `conversation.message.delta` carrying a ChatV3MessageDetail whose content is only the new fragment; any standard SSE client (EventSource, sseclient, eventsource-parser) parses it, an OpenAI client cannot, and adapting it is a ~20-line parser filtering on `event === 'conversation.message.delta'`.

CORRECTED: The streaming mechanism is real and genuinely incremental — every line of the offered evidence chain checks out in the Apache-2.0 source — but the claim overstates its uniformity across the four endpoints and its client compatibility, in three ways that would break the described ~20-line parser.

(1) `/api/conversation/chat` does NOT emit `conversation.message.delta`. Its own pull loop, `backend/application/conversation/agent_run.go:113` `(*ConversationApplicationService).pullStream`, collapses ack, `RunEventMessageDelta` and `RunEventMessageCompleted` into a single wire event named `run.RunEventMessage` — and `backend/api/model/conversation/run/run.go:39` defines that as the literal string `"message"`; the terminator is `run.RunEventDone` = `"done"`, not `conversation.stream.done`. Deltas and the final snapshot are therefore indistinguishable by event name on the internal path; a consumer must read `is_finish` / `index` out of the payload (`buildARSM2Message`, agent_run.go:154/170), and the payload is a nested `{message:{…}, is_finish, index, seq_id}` envelope, not a bare `ChatV3MessageDetail`. Only `/v3/chat` (`openapi_agent_run.go:356`) uses the `conversation.*` names and the flat `ChatV3MessageDetail`.

(2) The two workflow endpoints do not use hertz-contrib/sse at all, and they DO emit `id:`. `backend/api/handler/coze/workflow_service.go` imports `github.com/cloudwego/hertz/pkg/protocol/sse` (hertz core, `sse.Writer` / `Event{Type,ID,Data}` / `w.Write`), and both `sendStreamRunSSE` (line 948) and `sendChatFlowStreamRunSSE` (line 1119) set `ID:` — `msg.ID` and a monotonic `seq` respectively. So "no `id:` or `retry:` emitted" and "frame shape produced by hertz-contrib/sse v0.1.0" hold for `/v3/chat` and `/api/conversation/chat` only.

(3) Browser `EventSource` cannot consume any of these. All four are POST with a JSON body, and `/v1`+`/v3` sit behind `middleware.OpenapiAuthMW()` (`backend/api/middleware/openapi_auth.go:113`) requiring a Bearer PAT header — EventSource is GET-only with no custom headers. Coze's own frontend proves the point: `frontend/packages/common/chat-area/chat-core/package.json:50` depends on `eventsource-parser` and drives it from a fetch body (`src/channel/http-chunk/`). A parser works; the EventSource transport does not.

What IS confirmed exactly as stated, read at commit fefb05f: Apache-2.0, no additional terms (`LICENSE-APACHE` only; GitHub reports `apache-2.0`) — no cloud/enterprise gate on any of this. `ChatV3` (`agent_run_service.go:118-126`) sets `X-Accel-Buffering: no` and hands `sseImpl.NewSSESender(sse.NewStream(c))` to the application layer before the turn runs; the sync path is taken only on `req.Stream != nil && !*req.Stream` (line 108), so `stream` does default to true. `SSenderImpl.Send` is a one-line passthrough to `Stream.Publish`; hertz-contrib/sse v0.1.0 `Publish` is `Encode(c.w, event)` then `c.w.Flush()`, and `NewStreamWithWriter` sets `Content-Type: text/event-stream` + `Cache-Control: no-cache`; `Encode` writes id/event/retry only when non-zero, and `writeData` terminates with `\n\n`. `openapi_agent_run.go:356` `pullStream` is a bare `Recv()` loop with no batching or timer. The decisive producer line is present verbatim: `singleagent_run.go:345` `sendAnswerMsg.Content = streamMsg.Content` (fragment), with `fullContent` used only at EOF for `handlerAnswer` persistence (line 291) — an incremental stream, not a growing snapshot. All three tool caveats are accurate: `len(streamMsg.ToolCalls) > 0` sets `isToolCalls` (303) and `if isToolCalls { break }` at EOF (284) skips finalise, so the tool round trip emits nothing; `MessageTypeToolMidAnswer` (167) pumps `ToolMidAnswer.Recv()` and tags `Ext["message_title"]` from `Extra["workflow_node_name"]` (205-211); `exit.go:63` and `emitter.go:60` read `n.Data.Inputs.StreamingOutput` and set `RequireStreamingInput` true or false accordingly, so a chatflow whose End node has the toggle off returns one whole event. `preCreateAnswer` ends in a synchronous `crossmessage.DefaultSVC().PreCreate` (message_builder.go:221). Reasoning deltas do share the answer message's `id` (`modelAnswerMsg.ID = reasoningMsg.ID`, line 337), so filtering on `reasoning_content` — as the claim says — is the only way to separate them. No `chat.completion` string exists anywhere in `backend/`, so the OpenAI-incompatibility point stands.

Two field notes the claim's evidence glosses: the cited nginx block does set `proxy_read_timeout 600s; # …600s for SSE` (docker/nginx/conf.d/default.conf:32), but the same `location ~ ^/(api|v[1-3]|admin)` block also runs `sub_filter` with `sub_filter_once off` and `sub_filter_types 'application/json' 'text/event-stream'` — the reference deployment pipes the SSE body through a substitution filter, which the claim presents as "configured for it too". And the delta's `ChatV3MessageDetail` also carries `bot_id`, `meta_data`, `created_at`, `section_id` beyond the fields listed. Issue evidence is consistent with a working backend stream, not a broken one: coze-dev/coze-studio#2260 reports "console log IS streaming, the chat component renders it all at once" (a dev-mode UI bug), and #2449 / #2736 exist precisely because the delta rate is high enough to spike frontend INP.

REASONING: I read the actual source at a local checkout of coze-dev/coze-studio (commit fefb05ff27be1da939612fbf9faf5db62583b8ae) plus hertz-contrib/sse v0.1.0 at its release tag, and every link in the offered evidence chain is accurate — including the one line that matters, `sendAnswerMsg.Content = streamMsg.Content`, which distinguishes real fragments from a growing snapshot. So the capability is not marketing and is not tier-gated. It is overstated on scope, however. The internal `/api/conversation/chat` endpoint emits `event:message` / `event:done`, not `conversation.message.delta` / `conversation.stream.done`, and packs delta and final snapshot behind the same event name with an `is_finish` flag — the claim's parser would either fail or duplicate text there. The two workflow endpoints use a different SSE library (hertz core's `pkg/protocol/sse`, not hertz-contrib) and set `ID`, so `id:` is emitted, contradicting the stated frame shape. And browser EventSource cannot reach any of these endpoints at all, since all are POST-with-body behind a Bearer PAT; Coze's own frontend uses `eventsource-parser` over a fetch body, which is the correct narrower statement.


## Windmill

```json
{
 "name": "Windmill",
 "repo_url": "https://github.com/windmill-labs/windmill",
 "license_spdx": "AGPL-3.0-only AND Apache-2.0 AND LicenseRef-Windmill-Enterprise (GitHub's own API reports `NOASSERTION` for the repo license)",
 "license_is_osi_approved": true,
 "license_restrictions": "The parts we would use are OSI-approved, but the root `LICENSE` (34 lines, read verbatim) draws three restricting lines and the artifact the compose file actually pulls sits on the wrong side of one of them.\n\nQuoting `/LICENSE`:\n\n- \"The files under backend/ are AGPLv3 Licensed, except any snippets of code under the compile flag \"enterprise\". Those snippets and files are under a proprietary and commercial license. The files under frontend/ are AGPLv3 Licensed, except any snippets of code that require a positive license check to be activated.\"\n- \"Private and public forks MUST not include any of the above proprietary and commercial code.\"\n- \"The binary compilable from source code in this repository without the \"enterprise\" feature flag is open-source under the AGPLv3 License terms and conditions.\"\n- On the shipped image: \"Windmill Labs, Inc. grants a right to use all the features of the \"Community Edition\" for free without restrictions other than the limits and quotas set in the software and a right to distribute the community edition as is but **not to sell, resell, serve as a managed service, modify or wrap under any form without an explicit agreement**.\"\n\nSelf-hosted commercial internal use: permitted. Under AGPL, an unmodified internal deployment has no source-offer obligation to create, and five internal users for a month is not a \"managed service\". `python-client/ go-client/ powershell-client/` and the OpenFlow spec are Apache-2.0; `chat-sdk/package.json` declares `\"license\": \"Apache-2.0\"`.\n\nThe real snag is the CE-image clause versus what `.env` ships: `WM_IMAGE=ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill:main` is the Community Edition image, and `backend/Cargo.toml` builds it as `ce_core = [\"oss_core\", \"private\", \"operator\"]`. Standing a Node service in front of that image to relay ConversationRelay traffic is defensible as \"use\", but it is close enough to \"wrap under any form\" that it is a legal question, not an engineering one. Building `--features oss` from source instead puts us cleanly under AGPL \u2014 at the cost of the closed modules described below.\n\nFiles that disagree: `NOTICE` directs the reader to \"LICENSE-ALPH\", a file that does not exist in the repo (`LICENSE-AGPL` does), and `NOTICE` describes only \"Apache License Version 2.0 or the GNU Affero General Public License\" without mentioning the proprietary tier that `LICENSE` spends two paragraphs on. `frontend/LICENSE`, `backend/LICENSE`, `go-client/LICENSE`, `python-client/LICENSE` exist as per-directory copies.",
 "enterprise_gated_paths": "The AI agent and flow features themselves are on the AGPL side \u2014 I grepped `windmill-worker/src/ai_executor.rs`, `windmill-worker/src/ai/*.rs` and `windmill-ai/src/*.rs` for `cfg(feature = \"enterprise\")` and `cfg(not(feature = \"enterprise\"))` and got **zero hits**. The agent step, its tool loop, its streaming events and its memory are all in the open-source build. That is the good news and it is load-bearing.\n\nTwo capabilities we need are gated, and one of them is exactly the shape round one found:\n\n1. **The stream's poll cadence.** `windmill-api/src/jobs.rs`, inside `start_job_update_sse_stream`, the tunable floor is compiled out of the open build:\n```rust\nif let Some(poll_delay_ms) = poll_delay_ms {\n    #[cfg(feature = \"enterprise\")]\n    if poll_delay_ms < 50 { ... ms_duration = 50; } else { ms_duration = poll_delay_ms; }\n\n    #[cfg(not(feature = \"enterprise\"))]\n    tracing::warn!(\"Settable poll delay requires EE\");\n}\n```\nOn an AGPL build the parameter is accepted, logged as unsupported, and silently ignored. This is the single most consequential gate for requirement 2.\n\n2. **OTLP trace export.** `otel` is a member of `ee_core`, not of `oss_core` or `ce_core` (`backend/Cargo.toml`). `windmill-common/src/tracing_init.rs` gates the tracer on `#[cfg(all(feature = \"otel\", feature = \"enterprise\"))]`, and \u2014 decisively \u2014 the public-source implementation in `windmill-common/src/otel_oss.rs` is a stub that cannot ever return a tracer:\n```rust\npub(crate) fn init_otlp_tracer(_mode: &Mode, _hostname: &str, _env: &str)\n    -> Option<opentelemetry_sdk::trace::SdkTracer> { None }\n```\n`set_span_parent` is likewise `{}`. The real implementations live behind `#[cfg(feature = \"private\")] pub use crate::otel_ee::*;` and **`windmill-common/src/otel_ee.rs` does not exist in this repository**. So no feature-flag combination available from public source produces OTLP export.\n\nThe \"imported unconditionally at boot\" shape round one flagged is present here at build level rather than import level, and it is worth naming plainly: `ce_core = [\"oss_core\", \"private\", \"operator\"]` means **the published Community Edition image links closed-source modules whose sources are absent from the repo**. `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill:main` is therefore not reproducible from what we can read, and auditing what it does is not possible for us. `windmill_indexer` in the compose file is commented \"an EE feature\" and ships at `replicas: 0`; `ENABLE_MULTIPLAYER` is marked \"(Enterprise Edition)\". Neither is a capability we need.",
 "self_host": "Yes, genuinely, with no hosted control plane. `docker-compose.yml` at the repo root brings up `postgres:18`, `windmill_server`, `windmill_worker`, `windmill_worker_native`, `windmill_indexer` (at `replicas: 0`, EE), `windmill_extra` (LSP / multiplayer / debugger, individually toggled) and `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/caddy-l4`. The only required external dependency is Postgres, which is in the file. No license key is needed to boot, and no callout to Windmill Labs is required for the agent features to run.\n\nTwo caveats for our stack specifically. The compose file's Caddy service duplicates the reverse-proxy role our Traefik box already fills, so it would be dropped and the prefixes routed directly. And `.env` defaults to the CE image; a clean-AGPL deployment means building `--features oss` ourselves, which is a Rust build we would then own and which loses the closed `private` modules.",
 "streams_token_level": true,
 "streaming_transport": "One mechanism, reached through three endpoints. All three call the same function.\n\n- `GET /api/w/{workspace}/jobs_u/getupdate_sse/{job_id}` \u2014 `windmill-api/src/jobs.rs`, `get_job_update_sse`, registered at `.route(\"/getupdate_sse/{id}\", get(get_job_update_sse))`. This is what the official `windmill-chat` npm client uses (`chat-sdk/src/api.ts:127`).\n- The \"SSE stream webhook\" run-and-follow endpoint (same file, ~line 8203).\n- An HTTP route trigger in *Sync SSE* mode (`windmill-api/src/triggers/http/handler.rs:830`).\n\nEvery one of them constructs its body identically and delegates to `start_job_update_sse_stream`, so there is no second, better transport hiding behind the docs' three different names for it.\n\nFrame shape is a two-layer envelope, and this is the part that matters for us. The wire frame is legal SSE \u2014 `format!(\"data: {}\\n\\n\", ...)` with `Content-Type: text/event-stream`, `Cache-Control: no-cache` and `X-Accel-Buffering: no`. But the `data:` payload is a `JobUpdateSSEStream` JSON object (`windmill-api-sse/src/lib.rs`), tagged `{\"type\": \"update\" | \"error\" | \"notfound\" | \"timeout\" | \"ping\"}`, and the model's tokens are a **newline-delimited JSON blob nested inside its `new_result_stream` string field**, paired with a `stream_offset` cursor.\n\nThe inner NDJSON events are, verbatim from `chat-sdk/src/stream.ts` (and matching `StreamingEvent` in `backend/windmill-ai/src/types.rs`):\n```\n{ type: 'token_delta'; content: string }\n{ type: 'reasoning_token_delta'; content: string }\n{ type: 'tool_call'; call_id: string; function_name: string }\n{ type: 'tool_call_arguments'; call_id: string; function_name: string; arguments: string }\n{ type: 'tool_execution'; call_id: string; function_name: string }\n{ type: 'tool_result'; call_id: string; function_name: string; result: string; success: boolean }\n```\n\nA standard SSE client can read the frames. **Nothing OpenAI-shaped can parse them** \u2014 there is no `choices[].delta`, no `data: [DONE]`, and the token text is two JSON layers deep. A custom splitter is mandatory, and it must be incremental: `chat-sdk/src/stream.ts` documents that \"A chunk is not guaranteed to end on a line boundary, so an incomplete last line waits for the next `push`\", and the official docs repeat it \u2014 \"The `new_result_stream` field of an SSE event can contain multiple payloads at a time, make sure to split on line breaks.\" That splitter is ~60 lines and already written for us in Apache-2.0 code we could vendor.",
 "streaming_evidence": "Source, read from a clone of `main` at v1.813.0, not marketing copy.\n\nProducer side \u2014 real per-token emission, genuinely interleaved with tool calls. `backend/windmill-ai/src/sse.rs` sends `StreamingEvent::TokenDelta { content }` at roughly twenty call sites across the OpenAI, Anthropic, Google and Bedrock provider paths, one per provider delta, and emits `ToolCall` / `ToolCallArguments` / `ToolExecution` / `ToolResult` into the same ordered sink. `backend/windmill-worker/src/ai/stream_event_processor.rs` is the sink: `impl StreamEventSink for StreamEventProcessor` serialises each event, appends `\\n`, and pushes it onto a `tokio::sync::mpsc::channel::<String>(100)` whose consumer calls `append_result_stream` per event with a monotonically incrementing `offset`. So partial model output does leave the model loop one token at a time, during a turn containing tool calls. That is what makes the boolean true.\n\nExit path \u2014 and where it stops being token-level. `append_result_stream` (`backend/windmill-worker/src/job_logger.rs:72`) writes each event **to Postgres** (`Connection::Sql`) or POSTs it to `/api/w/{w}/agent_workers/push_result_stream/{job}` (`Connection::Http`). Nothing pushes onward. The reader is a polling loop in `start_job_update_sse_stream` (`windmill-api/src/jobs.rs`), whose own comment reads `// Poll for updates every 1 second` and whose actual cadence is:\n```rust\nlet mut ms_duration = if i > 100 || !fast.unwrap_or(false) { 3000 }\n                      else if i > 10 { 500 }\n                      else { 100 };\n```\nSo the model\u2192caller path is: model \u2192 worker mpsc \u2192 one Postgres row-append per token \u2192 poll \u2192 SSE. Incremental, yes. Token-level at the network boundary, no.\n\nFlow-position limit, from source rather than the doc: `StreamNotifier::update_flow_status_with_stream_job_inner` in `backend/windmill-worker/src/common.rs` sets `flow_status.stream_job` only when `should_set_stream_job` is true, and that value comes from `check_if_last_step(...)` or, when the flow declares `early_return`, `check_if_early_return_or_last_in_early_return_parent(...)`. No other step's stream is ever surfaced.\n\nCorroborating spec, agreeing with the source: \"If the AI agent step is not the last step of your flow, make sure to configure early return with the step's id to have the stream returned\" (windmill.dev/docs/core_concepts/ai_agents), and \"The stream exists while the job is running, and the full content becomes the result once the job completes\" (windmill.dev/docs/core_concepts/streaming).",
 "streaming_caveats": "Five, and the first is the one that fails requirement 2.\n\n**1. The delivery window is a database poll, and its tuning knob is EE-only.** Re-reading the ladder above with `fast=true` (which `chat-sdk/src/api.ts:122` hardcodes: `{ fast: 'true', only_result: 'true' }`): polls 1\u201310 at 100 ms, polls 11\u2013100 at **500 ms**, everything after at **3000 ms**. The first second of a turn is therefore delivered in 100 ms batches and everything after it in 500 ms batches. `poll_delay_ms` would fix this \u2014 the EE build clamps it to a 50 ms floor \u2014 but on an AGPL build the parameter is ignored with `tracing::warn!(\"Settable poll delay requires EE\")`. For ConversationRelay this means synthesis receives ~500 ms clumps of text rather than a token stream: prosody suffers, and barge-in responsiveness is bounded below by the poll window rather than by the model. Worse, the HTTP-route Sync SSE surface passes `poll_delay_ms` as literal `None` (`triggers/http/handler.rs`), so even an EE license could not tune that one.\n\n**2. Only the terminal node streams.** Confirmed in `check_if_last_step`, not merely documented. An agent step mid-flow emits nothing to the caller unless the flow sets `early_return` to that step's id \u2014 and `early_return` is a single node id, so exactly one step per flow can stream. Any post-agent orchestration we might want (logging, a hand-off decision, a Twilio call) must either move ahead of the agent or forfeit streaming. That is the \"post-processing disables the stream\" shape, and here it is structural rather than a flag.\n\n**3. The stream dies at 60 seconds.** `TIMEOUT_SSE_STREAM` defaults to `\"60\"` (`windmill-api-sse/src/lib.rs:339`); on expiry the server sends `JobUpdateSSEStream::Timeout` and returns. Reconnection is the client's job, resuming from `stream_offset` (`chat-sdk/src/follow.ts`). A voice call outlives this constantly, so our relay would have to own reconnect-and-resume. Keepalive `Ping` frames every >5 s do keep intermediaries open in the meantime.\n\n**4. One Postgres write per token.** `append_result_stream` is called once per `StreamingEvent` with a 20-second timeout, and on timeout the processor logs and `break`s \u2014 abandoning the rest of the stream. At a few hundred tokens per turn across concurrent calls this is a write-amplification story on the same Postgres that runs the queue, and the failure mode is a silently truncated answer mid-sentence.\n\n**5. Two coalescing layers, both invisible.** `JobUpdate::hash_str()` suppresses byte-identical consecutive updates, and the 100-slot mpsc between the model loop and the DB writer will backpressure the provider read if writes fall behind. Neither surfaces to the client as anything but a gap.",
 "tools_in_builder": "Yes \u2014 genuinely authored in the UI, not definition-only. This is Windmill's strongest answer to any of our requirements and the reason it is worth keeping on the list despite everything above.\n\nMechanism: an AI agent step carries a `tools` array of `FlowModule`s. When a tool's `value.type` is `RawScript`, its source code lives inside the flow value itself and is written in the flow editor's code pane. `backend/windmill-worker/src/ai/tools.rs` handles it at the `FlowModuleValue::RawScript` arm, synthesising a path of `format!(\"{}/tools/{}\", ctx.job.runnable_path(), tool_module.id)`. Tools may equally point at a deployed workspace script, a Hub script, a nested AI agent, or an MCP server tool. Official docs: \"Inline scripts - Write custom tools directly within the flow.\"\n\nExecutor: each tool call is pushed as its **own Windmill job**, run by the normal language executor for its `ScriptLang` \u2014 Bun/Deno TypeScript, Python, Go, Bash and the rest of the 20-odd languages, with `# native` TS running in-process via `windmill-runtime-nativets`. `tools.rs` notes tool jobs \"are pushed with the parent agent job's tag and executed inline on the\" same worker. Tool results come back through `ToolResult { call_id, function_name, result, success }` on the same stream as the tokens, so a partial answer, a tool invocation and the continuation all arrive over one connection.\n\nIsolation, stated plainly because the default is not what a reader would assume: `JobIsolationLevel` offers `None` / `Unshare` (PID namespace) / `NsjailSandboxing`, defaulting to `Undefined`, which falls through to env vars \u2014 and `DISABLE_NSJAIL` **defaults to `true`**:\n```rust\npub static ref DISABLE_NSJAIL: bool = std::env::var(\"DISABLE_NSJAIL\")\n    .ok().and_then(|x| x.parse::<bool>().ok()).unwrap_or(true);\n```\n(`backend/windmill-worker/src/worker.rs:371`). Out of the box, browser-authored tool code runs as an ordinary child process on the worker, sharing its filesystem and dependency cache. Turning nsjail on is a global setting plus a working `nsjail` binary, which the standard images do carry.",
 "sandbox_risk": "Browser-authored code executes as a real OS process on the worker, unsandboxed by default (`DISABLE_NSJAIL` \u2192 `true`, above), sharing the worker filesystem, `worker_dependency_cache` volume and \u2014 via `DATABASE_URL` in the compose environment \u2014 network reach to Postgres. Anyone who can edit a flow in the workspace has, in effect, code execution on the worker host. For a five-user internal demo that is an acceptable posture, and it is the same posture our own scaffold has; it is only worth stating because Windmill's UI invites non-engineers into the surface that grants it.\n\nFour real advisories from `GET /repos/windmill-labs/windmill/security-advisories`, with identifiers as requested:\n\n- **GHSA-2jfj-x8j7-7mfh** (high, **no CVE id assigned**) \u2014 \"Under nsjail security sandboxing, DuckDB scripts can escape the fs sandbox and poison shared worker caches for cross-workspace RCE.\" Note the direction of this one: it is a defeat of the strongest isolation mode, reached through the shared cache volume the compose file mounts.\n- **CVE-2026-29059** (high) \u2014 \"SUPERADMIN_SECRET (rarely used) can be accessed publicly on versions < 1.603.3 using RCE.\" Fixed well before v1.813.0.\n- **CVE-2026-33881** (low) \u2014 \"Rogue Workspace Admins can inject code via unescaped workspace environment variable interpolation in NativeTS executor.\" NativeTS is the in-process executor an inline tool would plausibly use.\n- **CVE-2026-54136** (low) \u2014 \"Resource-scoped API tokens can read script contents outside their allowed path via scripts/list_search.\"\n\nPlus one non-CVE exposure specific to how we would use it, documented by Windmill itself in `docs/reusable-ai-agents.md`: testing a flow inlines a linked agent's config into the preview job's `raw_flow`, so \"an agent's prompt and tool set are readable by whoever can read that preview job, which is a wider set than whoever can read the resource\". Credentials do not travel (the provider stays a `$res:` reference), but prompts do.",
 "prompt_versioning": "Yes for both prompt and tool selection, together, and git-trackable \u2014 but only on the inline path. Choosing the reusable path buys live propagation and pays for it with the exact unversioned-live-edit problem the lead warned about.\n\n**Inline agent (recommended for us).** The system prompt, provider, model, temperature, output schema, memory config, the tool roster *and each inline tool's source code* all live in one `FlowModuleValue::AIAgent` inside the flow value. Deploying appends a `flow_version` row, so prompt and tool selection are versioned as a single atomic artifact. `docs/reusable-ai-agents.md` confirms the runtime consequence: \"Inline (unlinked) agents are unaffected: their tools live in the flow value, which is snapshotted with the run.\" Roll back = redeploy an earlier version. Activating a change **requires a flow deploy**, not a redeploy of our Node service \u2014 which is the property we actually want.\n\n**Git-trackable, with a real DSL export.** The OpenFlow spec is `openflow.openapi.yaml` at the repo root and is **Apache-2.0** per `LICENSE` (\"The openapi files, including the OpenFlow spec is Apache 2.0 Licensed\"), so the format is not encumbered. The `wmill` CLI extracts inline tool code to standalone files on sync \u2014 `cli/windmill-utils-internal/src/inline-scripts/extractor.ts` branches on `m.value.type == \"aiagent\"`, walks `m.value.tools`, and for each `toolValue.tool_type === 'flowmodule' && toolValue.type === 'rawscript'` emits a real file, leaving a `!inline <path>` pointer in the YAML that `extractCurrentMapping` reads back. So a flow round-trips to a reviewable tree of `flow.yaml` + one file per tool. Prompt diffs and tool-code diffs land in the same commit and the same PR.\n\n**Reusable agent resource (the trap).** Saving an agent as an `ai_agent` resource gets you `resource_version` history \u2014 `docs/reusable-ai-agents.md`: \"an agent's prompt, model and tool set can be diffed and restored from the resource editor's History drawer\", append-only, up to 100 versions retained. But resolution is not pinned, and the doc is unusually candid about it:\n\n> \"A linked step resolves its agent resource when the step runs \u2014 that is what makes an edit propagate to every linked flow. It also means a run is not a snapshot: editing the agent while a flow is in-flight affects steps that have not started yet. The same applies one level down, where the effect is sharper: a *nested* agent tool of a linked agent runs as its own job and looks its definition up in the resource again by tool id, so an edit landing between the LLM selecting that tool and the tool starting can run the changed definition, or fail if the tool was removed.\"\n\nTwo further sharp edges on that path. Version history \"captures the resource, not its transitive closure\", so two byte-identical versions can behave differently if a referenced `$var:`/`$res:` changed underneath. And a linked step carries `tools: []`, so \"no dependency job ever visits the `ai_agent` resource\" \u2014 inline tool code inside an agent keeps whatever lock the authoring step had, script-path tools \"are invisible to redeploy cascades\", and dependencies resolve at job time. That is a worse versioning story than what we have in Langfuse today.\n\n**Surface.** Public and documented \u2014 flows, flow versions and resources are all in `backend/windmill-api/openapi.yaml`, with generated clients in `typescript-client/`, `python-client/`, `go-client/` and `rust-client/`. Not an internal route.",
 "otel_export": "No. Windmill would sit beside Langfuse, not replace it, and on a public-source build it cannot even feed it.\n\nNative OTLP export does not exist in anything we can build. `windmill-common/src/tracing_init.rs` gates the tracer on `#[cfg(all(feature = \"otel\", feature = \"enterprise\"))]`, `otel` belongs to `ee_core` rather than `oss_core`/`ce_core`, and the only `init_otlp_tracer` present in the repository returns `None` unconditionally:\n```rust\npub(crate) fn init_otlp_tracer(_mode: &Mode, _hostname: &str, _env: &str)\n    -> Option<opentelemetry_sdk::trace::SdkTracer> { None }\n```\nThe functioning implementation is re-exported from `crate::otel_ee` under `#[cfg(feature = \"private\")]`, and `windmill-common/src/otel_ee.rs` **is not in this repository**. Same story for the metric recorders (`otel_incr_queue_push_count` and friends are all `{}`) and for `set_span_parent`, which is `{}`.\n\nGENERATION spans: no. I grepped the whole backend for `gen_ai` and got **zero hits** \u2014 no `gen_ai.system`, no `gen_ai.request.model`, no `gen_ai.client.operation.time_to_first_chunk`. Nothing in Windmill emits the semantic-convention LLM span our current latency work depends on, so the one-query \"is it us or the model?\" answer we rely on today would be gone. Even under an EE licence, the OTel surface described is job/queue-level infrastructure tracing, not LLM observability, and I found no evidence of prompt or completion payloads on spans either way.\n\nWhat it does keep, in Postgres and its own run viewer: per-job rows in `v2_job`, `job_logs`, `flow_status`, the full `wm_stream` on the completed result, and \u2014 from `docs/ai-agent-evals.md` \u2014 an eval harness that \"records the version its agent was at when the run was enqueued, which is what makes a result attributable to a prompt state\". That is genuinely useful prompt-vs-outcome attribution. It is read through Windmill's UI and API, not exported, and it is not a trace.",
 "accepts_traceparent": "Partly, and not on the endpoint that matters \u2014 a migration would produce two disconnected trees.\n\nThe **streaming** endpoints read no trace context at all. `get_job_update_sse` extracts only `OptViewToken`, `OptAuthed`, `OptTokened`, the path and `JobUpdateQuery`; there is no header inspection anywhere in it or in `start_job_update_sse_stream`. Structurally there is nowhere for it to go: following a turn is a separate `GET` issued after the run `POST`, so even a correct header on the SSE request would describe the wrong span.\n\nThe **run/push** endpoint does accept one. `windmill-api/src/args.rs:607`:\n```rust\nfn inbound_traceparent(headers: &HeaderMap) -> Option<String> {\n    if !OTEL_TRACING_ENABLED.load(Ordering::Relaxed) { return None; }\n    headers.get(\"traceparent\").and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok()).map(|s| s.to_string())\n}\n```\nThe captured value is stashed as the reserved arg `_wm_traceparent` (`windmill-types/src/jobs.rs:722`), with any caller-supplied value in the body stripped first so only the real header wins. `windmill-worker/src/worker.rs:1052` then validates it with a strict `valid_w3c_traceparent` (rejects version `ff`, requires lowercase hex) and exports `TRACEPARENT`, `OTEL_TRACE_ID` and `OTEL_SPAN_ID` into the job's environment; `worker_flow.rs:4351` propagates it down to child flow jobs.\n\nBut \"continue the trace into its own spans\" fails on two counts in an open build. `OTEL_TRACING_ENABLED` is `AtomicBool::new(std::env::var(\"OTEL_TRACING\").is_ok())` \u2014 settable \u2014 yet with no tracer there are no spans to parent; and `set_span_parent`, the function that would do the parenting, is an empty stub in `otel_oss.rs`. The comment at `args.rs:359` states the intent \u2014 \"Read back at root-job completion to link the job's OTLP span to the originating distributed trace\" \u2014 and `add_root_flow_job_to_otlp` is likewise `{}`.\n\nNet effect for us: TAC's span for a turn and Windmill's record of the same turn would share nothing but a correlation id we would have to plumb by hand. The trace context does reach the tool code as an env var, which is a usable hook if we instrument tools ourselves, so a single tree is reachable \u2014 by our code, not Windmill's.",
 "prompt_prefix_stability": "Stable, deliberately so, and the only candidate examined that treats this as a first-class concern. This is a genuine strength.\n\nThe system prompt is `messages[0]` verbatim from the step config, with no wrapper, no run id and no timestamp (`backend/windmill-worker/src/ai_executor.rs`):\n```rust\nlet mut messages = if let Some(system_prompt) = args.system_prompt.clone().filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {\n    vec![OpenAIMessage { role: \"system\".to_string(),\n                         content: Some(OpenAIContent::Text(system_prompt)), ..Default::default() }]\n} else { vec![] };\n```\n`prompt_cache_key` is derived from workspace + runnable path + step id and explicitly not from the run, and the comment above it is the clearest statement of intent I found in any candidate:\n\n> \"Keyed on the step, not the run: every run of this step opens with the same system prompt and tool definitions, and each agent-loop iteration extends the previous one's prefix. Above ~15 requests/minute one key starts missing again, which is a reason to split it further, never to make it per-run.\"\n\n`bounded_prompt_cache_key` folds an over-long key into a SHA-256 digest of itself while keeping a readable head, preserving \"same step still yields the same key across runs\". It is sent as `prompt_cache_key` on each request, with a documented fallback that drops it if a non-OpenAI-compatible provider rejects it (`rejects_prompt_cache_key`, retried without). Conversation history is appended after the system message, so growth is suffix-only \u2014 cache-friendly, the same shape our own turns have.\n\nThree ways it can still go cold, all of which we would control:\n\n- The system prompt is an input transform, so a JS expression interpolating a clock or a call SID would defeat the prefix. Authoring discipline, not a runtime defect \u2014 but the UI does not warn.\n- With a **linked reusable agent**, editing the agent changes the prefix mid-flight (live resolution, above) while `prompt_cache_key` \u2014 keyed on workspace/path/step \u2014 stays byte-identical. The result is a silent cache miss with no signal anywhere. Another argument for the inline path.\n- The comment's own caveat: one key starts missing above roughly 15 requests/minute. At demo volume this is irrelevant.\n\nNot established: whether tool definitions serialise in a stable order across runs. I read the emission of the system message and the cache key but did not trace the tool-array assembly far enough to prove ordering is deterministic; that is in `unverified`.",
 "owns_history": "Windmill owns it, in its own Postgres, keyed by its own id \u2014 and re-sends it to the provider every turn. Two histories drifting is a real risk here, and there is a clean escape hatch.\n\nThe id is `memory_id`, a UUID that doubles as the conversation id. `chat-sdk/src/api.ts:98` \u2014 \"Starts a turn: runs the flow with `memory_id` set to the conversation id.\" Precedence is resolved in `ai_executor.rs:1032`: \"Flow-level memory_id (from chat mode) takes precedence over step-level memory_id\", falling back to `Memory::Auto { memory_id, .. }`. Storage is `windmill-api-flow-conversations`, exposed as `GET /flow_conversations/list`, `GET /flow_conversations/{id}/messages` and `DELETE /flow_conversations/delete/{id}`; the worker writes turns through `add_message_to_conversation_tx` with a `MessageType`, and reads prior turns back via `read_from_memory(db, &job.workspace_id, memory_id, step_id)` at the top of each run. So yes: history is re-read from Postgres and re-sent in full to the provider on every turn.\n\nFor our architecture that means TAC would hold the caller's turns for ConversationRelay while Windmill independently accumulated its own copy \u2014 and the two would diverge the moment anything is injected on one side only. Concretely: TAC's memory Recall on turn 1, a barge-in that truncates an utterance TAC saw and Windmill recorded whole, or a human hand-off. Our own notes already record how easy that inference is to get wrong.\n\nThe escape hatch is real and I would take it. `Memory::Manual { messages }` bypasses Windmill's store entirely \u2014 `ai_executor.rs:1014`, `let use_manual_messages = matches!(args.memory, Some(Memory::Manual { .. }))`, and the validation immediately below requires \"at least one of memory with manual messages or user_message\". Passing TAC's history as manual messages keeps exactly one source of truth. The cost is losing chat mode's automatic threading and the `flow_conversations` UI, and \u2014 because manual mode bypasses `memory_id` \u2014 losing the server-side transcript the run viewer would otherwise show.\n\nOne flag on the `omit_output_from_conversation` parameter threaded through `run_agent`: it exists and is plainly about suppressing a turn's output from the stored conversation, but I did not trace its call sites, so I have not established when Windmill's stored history intentionally diverges from what the caller heard.",
 "embeddable_in_node": "No. This is a hard architectural mismatch with the seam we designed, and no packaging choice softens it.\n\nThe runtime is a Rust binary \u2014 `backend/` is a Cargo workspace of ~60 crates producing a `windmill` executable that runs as `MODE=server` or `MODE=worker` and requires Postgres for the job queue, the flow state machine and the result stream. There is no library entry point, no WASM target, no FFI surface intended for embedding. Even reading a token requires the round trip described above: a worker writes it to Postgres and a separate API process polls it back out. There is no in-process path from a model delta to our code.\n\nThe npm package is a **client**, not the runtime: `chat-sdk/package.json`, `\"name\": \"windmill-chat\"`, version `1.813.0`, Apache-2.0, described as \"Build chat interfaces on Windmill flows deployed in chat mode, from any frontend or raw app\", with zero runtime dependencies and optional peers on `react`, `ai` and `@assistant-ui/react`. Entry points are `windmill-chat`, `/react`, `/ai-sdk`, `/assistant-ui`. It speaks HTTP and SSE to a Windmill server over the wire.\n\nSo the integration would be: our Node service holds the ConversationRelay WebSocket, `POST`s each turn to Windmill, opens `getupdate_sse`, splits the nested NDJSON, and feeds `token_delta` contents into the voice channel's async-iterable method. Our public method still consumes an async iterable, so the seam itself survives untouched \u2014 which is the good news. What we would be adding is a network hop, a Postgres write per token, a 500 ms poll window and a 60 s reconnect, all inside the latency budget a caller judges. Worth restating against our own measured numbers: the framework we have today costs 84 ms end to end.\n\nUsefully, `windmill-chat`'s `/ai-sdk` export is a Vercel AI SDK `ChatTransport`, and `chat-sdk/src/stream.ts` is ~60 lines of Apache-2.0 incremental NDJSON parsing we could vendor directly rather than reimplement.",
 "visual_editing": "Strong \u2014 the best-in-class answer among the requirements, and the reason this is worth a second look if the streaming boundary ever moves.\n\nA non-engineer can edit a prompt without help. The AI agent step's editor exposes provider/model, system prompt, temperature, output schema and memory mode as ordinary form fields; the tool roster renders as display-only nodes on the flow graph, each with a section below the step's inputs carrying \"the standard schema-aware input editors (prop picker included) and a read-only view of its code\" (`docs/reusable-ai-agents.md`). Reusable agents get a dedicated editor opened over the flow, a *Draft* badge showing what a test would run, an Unlink button that forks the resolved config back into the step, and a History drawer with per-version diff and restore. Edits autosave to a per-user draft (`useAgentDraft`, `draft` table with `item_kind = 'resource'`) and land only on an explicit Deploy, with the confirmation dialog listing every linked agent carrying a draft. Deploy-time behaviour is well thought through: \"an undeployed draft trigger is deleted, because it belongs to the flow, while an agent draft belongs to a resource other flows also use.\"\n\nWiring a tool is a two-tier story. Picking one \u2014 a workspace script, a Hub script, an MCP server tool, a nested agent \u2014 is genuinely point-and-click, and binding its inputs is done with schema-aware editors and a prop picker over previous step results. *Authoring* a new tool means writing TypeScript or Python in the editor's code pane. So \"edit the prompt\" is fully non-technical; \"add a brand-new tool\" is not, though it is in-browser rather than in our repo, which is the requirement.\n\nTwo honest caveats. The draft being per-user means \"a flow test can behave differently for two people looking at the same flow\" \u2014 a support burden if several people iterate on one prompt. And the frontend is AGPL \"except any snippets of code that require a positive license check to be activated\", so some editor affordances may be license-gated; I did not enumerate which, and the AI agent step itself showed no such gate.",
 "maturity": "Very active, professionally run, and moving fast enough that anything pinned here should be re-checked before a decision.\n\n- Latest release **v1.813.0, published 2026-09-16** \u2014 today. Preceded by v1.812.0 (2026-09-15) and v1.811.1 (2026-09-13): effectively a daily release train, managed by release-please (`release-please-config.json`, `.release-please-manifest.json`).\n- **17,950 stars**; `pushed_at` 2026-09-16T17:04:30Z; GitHub reports the repo license as `NOASSERTION`, consistent with the split described above.\n- Commit cadence: **~350 commits in the last 30 days** (from the `Link: rel=\"last\"` header on `/commits?since=2026-08-16&per_page=1`), so roughly a dozen a day.\n- Images are published by the vendor, Windmill Labs, at `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill` (CE), `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill-ee` (EE), plus `windmill-extra` and a vendored `caddy-l4`. `.env` defaults to `:main` rather than a version tag, which is worth overriding.\n- Security process is real: five published advisories, four with CVE identifiers, fixes shipped ahead of disclosure.\n- Engineering signals in-repo are unusually good \u2014 `integration_tests/ai_agent_tests/`, an `ai_evals/` harness with fixtures, `backend/tests/otel.rs`, per-crate `AGENTS.md`, a CLA, and a `docs/` tree whose files (`reusable-ai-agents.md`, `ai-agent-evals.md`, `enterprise.md`) document known limitations candidly rather than selling. `docs/reusable-ai-agents.md` volunteering the live-resolution hazard, the preview-inlining information leak and the lockfile gap is the behaviour of a team that keeps honest notes.\n\nThe caution that goes with the velocity: the streaming internals we depend on are private implementation, not public contract. `StreamingEvent`, `new_result_stream`, `stream_offset` and the poll ladder could all change in a point release, and only the outer SSE envelope and the NDJSON split are described in the docs.",
 "fit_verdict": "REJECT for this architecture, on requirement 2, but by a much narrower margin than the other candidates and for a reason that is a build flag rather than a design flaw.\n\nRequirements 1 and 3 pass cleanly, and 3 passes better than anything else examined. Self-hosting is a real compose file with Postgres as its only dependency and no control plane. The AI agent step, its tool loop, its streaming events and its memory carry **zero** `cfg(feature = \"enterprise\")` \u2014 the agent features are on the AGPL side, which is the question the lead asked to settle, and the answer is favourable. Tool implementations are genuinely authored in the browser, run as first-class Windmill jobs in 20-odd languages, and \u2014 via `wmill` inline-script extraction \u2014 round-trip to a reviewable git tree where a prompt change and a tool-code change land in one commit. On two of the four things round one missed, Windmill is better than what we run today: the prompt prefix is byte-stable by explicit design, with a source comment reasoning about provider cache keys, and the flow-version path versions prompt and tool selection as one atomic artifact with a documented Apache-2.0 DSL.\n\nRequirement 2 fails at the network boundary, not in the model loop. Inside the worker, streaming is exactly what we need: per-token `token_delta` events interleaved with `tool_call` / `tool_execution` / `tool_result` on one ordered sink. Then every token is written to a Postgres row, and the only reader is a poll loop whose cadence is 100 ms for ten polls and **500 ms thereafter**, rising to 3000 ms past 46 seconds. The knob that fixes this \u2014 `poll_delay_ms`, clamped to a 50 ms floor \u2014 is compiled out of the open build and answers `tracing::warn!(\"Settable poll delay requires EE\")`. ConversationRelay would synthesise from ~500 ms clumps of text. Against the 84 ms our current framework costs end to end, that is not a regression we can absorb on the turn a caller judges.\n\nThree further costs compound it, none fatal alone. Only the flow's terminal step streams (`check_if_last_step`), and `early_return` names a single node, so exactly one step per flow can stream \u2014 any post-agent orchestration forfeits it. Streams terminate at 60 seconds (`TIMEOUT_SSE_STREAM`), so we would own reconnect-and-resume for the length of every call. And Windmill would sit beside Langfuse rather than retire it: `init_otlp_tracer` returns `None` unconditionally in public source, its real implementation is in an `otel_ee` module absent from the repo, and `gen_ai` appears nowhere \u2014 so `gen_ai.client.operation.time_to_first_chunk`, the attribute that makes \"is it us or the model?\" one query, would be gone.\n\nWhat would change the verdict, in order: upstream pushing stream events instead of polling them (or ungating `poll_delay_ms`); `early_return` accepting more than one node. If we ever paid for EE, requirement 2 becomes a 50 ms floor and OTLP export appears, at which point Windmill is a serious contender \u2014 but the brief is unpaid self-hosting, and on those terms it does not clear the bar. If it were adopted anyway, adopt inline agents rather than reusable ones, and pass TAC's history via `Memory::Manual` to keep one transcript.",
 "disqualifiers": [
  "REQUIREMENT 2, PRIMARY: token deltas reach a client only through a Postgres poll loop whose cadence is 100 ms for the first 10 polls, then 500 ms, then 3000 ms past ~46 s. `start_job_update_sse_stream` in windmill-api/src/jobs.rs. ConversationRelay would synthesise from ~500 ms text clumps, not a token stream.",
  "REQUIREMENT 2, AND THE PAID GATE THAT SEALS IT: the fix \u2014 `poll_delay_ms`, EE-clamped to a 50 ms floor \u2014 is `#[cfg(feature = \"enterprise\")]`; the open build accepts the parameter and ignores it with `tracing::warn!(\"Settable poll delay requires EE\")`. A capability we need sits behind the paid tier.",
  "REQUIREMENT 2: no push transport exists as an alternative. All three SSE surfaces \u2014 `getupdate_sse`, the run-and-follow webhook, and HTTP routes in Sync SSE mode \u2014 delegate to the same `start_job_update_sse_stream`. The HTTP-route surface passes `poll_delay_ms` as literal `None`, so even EE could not tune it.",
  "REQUIREMENT 2: only the flow's terminal step streams. `StreamNotifier::update_flow_status_with_stream_job_inner` sets `flow_status.stream_job` solely when `check_if_last_step` or the single `early_return` node id matches, so exactly one step per flow can stream and any post-agent orchestration forfeits streaming.",
  "One Postgres row-append per token (`append_result_stream`), on the same database that runs the job queue; on a 20 s write timeout the processor logs and `break`s, truncating the answer mid-sentence with no client-visible signal.",
  "Streams die at 60 s (`TIMEOUT_SSE_STREAM` default \"60\") with a `Timeout` frame; a voice call outlives this repeatedly, so reconnect-and-resume from `stream_offset` becomes our code's problem.",
  "Cannot retire Langfuse, or even feed it: `init_otlp_tracer` returns `None` unconditionally in public source, the working implementation lives in `windmill-common/src/otel_ee.rs` which is absent from the repository, and `otel` belongs to `ee_core`. `set_span_parent` and `add_root_flow_job_to_otlp` are empty stubs.",
  "No LLM observability semantics at all: zero occurrences of `gen_ai` in the entire backend. We would lose `gen_ai.client.operation.time_to_first_chunk`, the single attribute that makes latency attribution one query instead of an investigation.",
```
