### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Windmill is licensed AGPL-3.0-only AND Apache-2.0 AND LicenseRef-Windmill-Enterprise (GitHub API: NOASSERTION), OSI approved: true, and self-hosted commercial internal use of the capabilities we need is permitted with nothing behind a paid tier — with the only snag being that the CE image the compose file pulls sits on the wrong side of the "not to sell, resell, serve as a managed service, modify or wrap" clause; the AI agent/tool-loop/streaming/memory are all AGPL-side, while settable SSE poll cadence and OTLP export are enterprise-gated.

CORRECTED: Directionally right on the licence anatomy and on the agent core being open, but the headline is self-contradicting and the gating analysis puts the CE image on the wrong side of the line.

VERIFIED AS STATED. `/LICENSE` is 34 lines and all four quotes are verbatim (three-way split; "Private and public forks MUST not include any of the above proprietary and commercial code"; the no-`enterprise`-flag binary is AGPLv3; the CE-image grant is "free without restrictions other than the limits and quotas set in the software … but not to sell, resell, serve as a managed service, modify or wrap under any form without an explicit agreement"). `NOTICE` does point at the non-existent `LICENSE-ALPH` while `LICENSE-AGPL` exists, and does omit the proprietary tier. `frontend/LICENSE`, `backend/LICENSE`, `go-client/LICENSE`, `python-client/LICENSE` all exist. `chat-sdk/package.json` declares `"license": "Apache-2.0"`. `docker-compose.yml` ships `windmill_indexer` at `replicas: 0` with the comment "an EE feature" and `ENABLE_MULTIPLAYER=false # … (Enterprise Edition)`. `.env` sets `WM_IMAGE=ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill:main` with the EE image commented out. `ce_core = ["oss_core", "private", "operator"]` is exact. And the agent core really is open: zero hits for `feature = "enterprise"`, `feature = "private"`, or any license check across `windmill-worker/src/ai_executor.rs`, `windmill-worker/src/ai/*.rs` and `windmill-ai/src/*.rs`.

REFUTED 1 — "nothing behind a paid tier" is false, and the claim's own body concedes it. There are 671 `#[cfg(feature = "enterprise")]` sites in `backend/`. Two of them are the capabilities named.

REFUTED 2 — the poll-cadence gate is framed as an AGPL-build cost, but it hits the CE image the compose file actually pulls. `.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:97` builds `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill` with `features=ce`; `ce → ce_rpi → ce_core = oss_core + private + operator`, and `enterprise` appears in none of them. Windmill's own PR #11134 says it plainly: "`pollDelayMs: 50` once the instance reports an enterprise license … a CE server ignores it and logs a warning per poll", matched by `frontend/src/lib/components/flows/conversations/FlowChat.svelte:59` (`pollDelayMs: $enterpriseLicense ? 50 : undefined`) and by `chat-sdk/src/types.ts`: "(Enterprise; 50 at the fastest, other servers ignore it). Unset, the server relaxes from 100 ms to 3 s over a long turn." So building `--features oss` from source costs nothing on either gated capability — both are already absent from CE. The gate is the paid EE image plus a runtime license, not the AGPL/CE line.

REFUTED 3 — the concrete cadence, which the claim omits and which matters more than the gate: streaming is DB-polled, not pushed. The worker writes each event to the job's result stream (`StreamEventProcessor` → `append_result_stream`, one row per event), and `stream_job` (`windmill-api/src/jobs.rs`) hands the job to `start_job_update_sse_stream` with `fast = Some(true)`, giving 100 ms for the first 10 polls (~1 s), 500 ms through poll 100 (~46 s), then 3 s. Deltas are incremental and parseable, but on CE they arrive in batches that get coarser exactly as an agent loop runs long. `openapi.yaml` documents `poll_delay_ms` as merely "delay between polling for job updates in milliseconds", with no EE note — the spec overstates it. Closed issue #10716 ("fall back to polling when a proxy mutes the job SSE stream") shows SSE delivery is not guaranteed through intermediaries either.

REFUTED 4 — the otel quote is truncated in a way that changes its meaning. The stub is `#[cfg(all(feature = "otel", feature = "enterprise", not(feature = "private")))]`, and `otel_oss.rs` opens with `#[cfg(feature = "private")] pub use crate::otel_ee::*;`. The conclusion (no OTLP from public source) holds, but for the CE image the operative reason is that neither `otel` nor `enterprise` is in `ce_core`, so the call site `#[cfg(all(feature = "otel", feature = "enterprise"))] init_otlp_tracer(...)` in `windmill-common/src/tracing_init.rs` compiles out entirely.

REFUTED 5 — the missing-source problem is systemic, not one file. `docs/enterprise.md` states the convention: "Source lives in `windmill-ee-private` (sibling repo), symlinked into each crate's `src/`" and "`_ee.rs` files are gitignored in the main windmill repo". There are 86 `mod *_ee;` declarations and zero `*_ee.rs` files present; `backend/ee-repo-ref.txt` pins the private repo at `d252afcc80e77fcc4f9a2a346b80908c8605a6c0`. So `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill:main` is unauditable for us, as claimed — but for 86 modules, not just `otel_ee.rs`.

REFUTED 6 — licence metadata details. `LicenseRef-Windmill-Enterprise` appears nowhere in the repository; it is an invented identifier. "OSI approved: true" is false for the artifact as a whole: GitHub reports `NOASSERTION` / spdx_id NOASSERTION, and the shipped CE image is governed by a bespoke non-OSI grant. `LICENSE`'s Apache list includes `deno-client/`, a directory that no longer exists; `powershell-client/` has no LICENSE copy; `typescript-client/package.json` declares `"license": "Apache 2.0"` (not a valid SPDX id) and neither it nor `chat-sdk/` nor `react-sdk/` appears in `LICENSE`'s Apache enumeration.

NOT REFUTED, and worth keeping: the licence reading for our use is sound. An unmodified internal AGPL deployment has no source-offer obligation to discharge, five internal users for a month is not a managed service, and standing a Node relay in front of the CE image is a "wrap under any form" question a lawyer answers, not an engineer. A source build with `--features oss` removes the ambiguity at no capability cost relative to CE for the two gates examined.

REASONING: Cloned windmill-labs/windmill at main and read the primary sources. The licence quotes, NOTICE defects, per-directory LICENSE files, compose/.env contents and `ce_core` definition all check out verbatim, and the AI agent grep result (zero enterprise/private cfgs, zero license checks in ai_executor.rs, windmill-worker/src/ai/, windmill-ai/src/) reproduces exactly. Three things break the claim. First, the headline "nothing behind a paid tier" is contradicted by its own body and by 671 enterprise cfg sites. Second, the poll-cadence gate is presented as a cost of choosing an AGPL build, but the published CE image is built `features=ce` (docker-image.yml:97) and `ce` never reaches `enterprise` — Windmill's own PR #11134 and `chat-sdk/src/types.ts` state that a CE server ignores `pollDelayMs` and warns per poll, so the image the compose file pulls is already on the gated side and `--features oss` costs nothing here. Third, the otel stub quote drops the `not(feature = "private")` cfg and the `pub use crate::otel_ee::*` re-export above it; the no-OTLP conclusion survives but by a different mechanism (otel/enterprise absent from ce_core, so the tracing_init call site compiles out), and the missing-source problem covers 86 `mod *_ee;` declarations pinned via ee-repo-ref.txt, not one file. Added the actual CE cadence (100 ms → 500 ms → 3 s) since that, not the gate, is the operative constraint for relaying deltas, plus the OpenAPI spec's silence about the EE requirement and issue #10716 on proxies muting the SSE stream. `LicenseRef-Windmill-Enterprise` does not exist anywhere in the repo, and "OSI approved: true" cannot hold for an artifact GitHub reports as NOASSERTION and whose shipped image carries a bespoke no-resell/no-wrap grant.

### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Windmill streams token-level model output incrementally during an agent turn that includes tool calls, via one mechanism reached through three endpoints that all delegate to `start_job_update_sse_stream`; frames are a two-layer envelope (SSE `data:` → `JobUpdateSSEStream` JSON → NDJSON `StreamingEvent`s in `new_result_stream`), unparseable by anything OpenAI-shaped, with a ~60-line Apache-2.0 splitter available to vendor; producer emits `TokenDelta` at ~20 call sites in `windmill-ai/src/sse.rs` interleaved with tool events; exit path is one Postgres row-append per token read by a 100/500/3000 ms poll ladder; only the terminal (or `early_return`) flow node streams; five caveats incl. EE-only `poll_delay_ms`, 60 s stream timeout, per-token DB writes, and two invisible coalescing layers.

CORRECTED: Directionally right and unusually well-sourced — the core boolean survives, and roughly 90% of the mechanism, line numbers and caveats check out verbatim against v1.813.0 (commit c3e11bd). But it is overstated in three ways that matter, and it omits a gate that could bite us.

WHAT IS CONFIRMED, verbatim at v1.813.0:
- Exactly three callers of `start_job_update_sse_stream` (defined `backend/windmill-api/src/jobs.rs:10212`), called at `jobs.rs:10172` (`get_job_update_sse`, routed at `jobs.rs:445` as `.route("/getupdate_sse/{id}", get(get_job_update_sse))`), `jobs.rs:8178` (`stream_job`, the SSE-webhook run-and-follow), and `backend/windmill-api/src/triggers/http/handler.rs:830` (`RequestType::SyncSse`). All three build the body with the identical `format!("data: {}\n\n", serde_json::to_string(&x)...)`. There is no second transport.
- Two-layer envelope: `JobUpdateSSEStream` is `#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "lowercase")]` over `Update|Error|NotFound|Timeout|Ping`; `JobUpdate` carries `new_result_stream: Option<String>` + `stream_offset: Option<i32>` (`backend/windmill-api-sse/src/lib.rs`). `StreamingEvent` is `#[serde(tag="type", rename_all="snake_case")]` with exactly the six variants quoted, and `chat-sdk/src/stream.ts` matches them field-for-field. No `choices[].delta`, no `[DONE]` — nothing OpenAI-shaped parses this.
- The splitter is 65 lines, and the quoted comment ("A chunk is not guaranteed to end on a line boundary…") is verbatim. `chat-sdk/package.json` declares `"license": "Apache-2.0"`, so vendoring is permitted.
- Poll ladder, EE gate and `tracing::warn!("Settable poll delay requires EE")` are exact (`jobs.rs:10333-10352`); `TIMEOUT_SSE_STREAM` default `"60"` is at `windmill-api-sse/src/lib.rs:339`; `append_result_stream` is at `windmill-worker/src/job_logger.rs:72`; the `mpsc::channel::<String>(100)`, the `\n` append, the monotonic `offset` and the 20-second-timeout-then-`break` are all in `windmill-worker/src/ai/stream_event_processor.rs`; `should_set_stream_job` / `check_if_last_step` / `check_if_early_return_or_last_in_early_return_parent` are as described (`windmill-worker/src/common.rs:1924-2060`); `chat-sdk/src/api.ts:122` really does hardcode `{ fast: 'true', only_result: 'true' }` and hits `getupdate_sse` at line 127. Both doc sentences exist verbatim in `windmilldocs-public` `docs/core_concepts/54_ai_agents/index.mdx`.

WHERE IT IS WRONG OR OVERSTATED:
1. "roughly twenty call sites" of `StreamingEvent::TokenDelta` is wrong by 4x. `backend/windmill-ai/src/sse.rs` at v1.813.0 has FIVE `TokenDelta` construction sites (lines 190, 415, 502, 649, 888 — one per provider path, which is the substantive point). ~20 is the count of ALL `StreamingEvent::*` constructions including `ReasoningTokenDelta` and the four tool variants, two of which are in tests (lines 1019, 1059). Also `TokenDelta` is one per provider *delta chunk*, not per token.
2. An undisclosed gate narrows the capability: `ai_executor.rs:1215` sets `*has_stream = user_wants_streaming && is_text_output`, where `is_text_output = output_type == &OutputType::Text` (line 1030). A non-text agent step gets `StreamEventProcessor::new_silent()` and streams NOTHING — no events reach the DB at all. Streaming is on by default (`streaming_requested` → `streaming.unwrap_or(true)`), but only for text output. The docs agree. So the boolean is true for text-output agent steps only.
3. The header trio is not uniform. Only `get_job_update_sse` sets `X-Accel-Buffering: no` (`jobs.rs:10204`). The SSE-webhook (`jobs.rs:8203`) sets Content-Type + Cache-Control + Connection; the HTTP-route Sync SSE (`handler.rs:857`) sets only Content-Type + Cache-Control. If we sit behind nginx, two of the three surfaces will buffer.
4. "one Postgres row-append per token" mis-describes the write. `append_result_stream_db` (`backend/windmill-common/src/result_stream.rs`) does `INSERT INTO job_result_stream_v2 (workspace_id, job_id, stream, idx) … ON CONFLICT (job_id, idx) DO UPDATE`, with a fresh `idx` per event — so it is one new ROW per event, not an append to one row. Worse than stated for row count, not better.
5. Caveat 2's "exactly one step per flow can stream" is right about `flow_status.stream_job`, but both `check_if_last_step` and `check_if_early_return_or_last_in_early_return_parent` walk UP through `is_branch_one` parents, so the streaming node can be the last step of a nested branch-one chain. And a client that already knows a step's own job id can open `getupdate_sse` directly on that job, bypassing the flow-level restriction.
6. The docs do NOT corroborate the incremental-splitter requirement. The sentence immediately after the one quoted reads "Only complete payloads are streamed, you do not need to handle partial JSON" — i.e. the docs claim the opposite of a mid-line chunk boundary. Only `chat-sdk/src/stream.ts` documents the partial-line case. The claim conflated SDK source with spec.

WHERE IT IS UNDERSTATED (caveat 1 is worse than written): `poll_delay_ms` is gated on the `enterprise` cargo cfg, and `backend/Cargo.toml:212-213` defines `ce = ["ce_rpi","jemalloc","dind","agent_worker_server"]` (no `enterprise`) while `ee = ["ce","ee_core","ee_server",…]` pulls it in; `.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:97` builds the official CE image with `features=ce`. So `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill` Community Edition ignores `poll_delay_ms` too — this is not merely a self-built-AGPL limitation. Independently corroborated by issue #8503 ("bug: SSE only sends update every 3 seconds", CE v1.663.0), whose resolution was `?fast=true`, i.e. the ladder is the only knob a non-EE user has.

NET FOR CONVERSATIONRELAY: the mechanism is real, AGPL, self-hostable, and genuinely interleaves tokens with tool events — but at the network boundary it delivers 100 ms batches for the first second and 500 ms batches thereafter, is unavailable for non-text agent output, needs our own reconnect-and-resume past 60 s, and needs our own NDJSON splitter. Requirement 2 (token-level at the wire) fails.

REASONING: I read the source at tag v1.813.0 (commit c3e11bd) rather than main, plus the LICENSE, the docs repo, the Cargo feature graph, the Docker workflow, and the issue tracker. The claim's structural assertions all hold — I found exactly three callers of `start_job_update_sse_stream`, the two-layer envelope, the poll ladder, the EE gate, the 60 s timeout, the per-event DB write, and the terminal-node restriction, all at or near the cited lines. Refuted on the "overstated counts as refuted" rule for four concrete errors: the ~20 TokenDelta call sites are 5; an undisclosed `is_text_output` gate silences streaming entirely for structured-output agent steps; `X-Accel-Buffering: no` is set on only one of the three endpoints; and the DB write inserts a new row per event rather than appending to one. Two further corrections: the docs actively contradict the incremental-splitter rationale ("Only complete payloads are streamed"), and the EE gate is stricter than described — the official Community Edition image is built `features=ce`, which excludes `enterprise`, so even the vendor's own CE build ignores `poll_delay_ms`. The licence claim survives: `chat-sdk/package.json` declares Apache-2.0, though the root LICENSE's Apache-2.0 enumeration does not list `chat-sdk/`, so the manifest is the operative grant.

### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Windmill allows tool definitions AND their implementations to be authored inside the builder rather than in our application source. Mechanism: an AI agent step carries a `tools` array of FlowModules; a `RawScript` tool's source lives in the flow value and is written in the flow editor's code pane; `backend/windmill-worker/src/ai/tools.rs` synthesises `format!("{}/tools/{}", ctx.job.runnable_path(), tool_module.id)`. Each tool call is pushed as its own Windmill job run by the normal language executor. Tool results come back through `ToolResult { call_id, function_name, result, success }` on the same stream as the tokens, so a partial answer, a tool invocation and the continuation all arrive over one connection. Isolation defaults to none: `DISABLE_NSJAIL` defaults to `true`.

CORRECTED: Substantially correct on authoring, execution and isolation — and none of it is tier-gated — but the streaming clause is overstated in three ways, one of which is the "terminal node only" failure mode.

VERIFIED AS STATED (windmill-labs/windmill @ main, sparse clone 2026-09-16):
- Inline authoring is real, not definition-only. `backend/windmill-worker/src/ai/tools.rs:387-408` is the `FlowModuleValue::RawScript` arm; when the module carries no path it synthesises exactly `format!("{}/tools/{}", ctx.job.runnable_path(), tool_module.id)` and hands `content` + `language` + `lock` to `raw_script_to_payload`. Sibling arms cover `Script` (deployed/Hub, :375), `FlowScript` (:410) and `AIAgent` (:429, capped at 2 levels of nesting with an explicit runtime error). MCP tools are a separate path (`McpToolSource`, :180-260).
- The docs quote is accurate: docs.windmill.dev/docs/core_concepts/ai_agents lists "Inline scripts - Write custom tools directly within the flow", alongside Workspace scripts and Hub scripts. Note the docs say tools come from *three* sources; nested AI agents and MCP servers are documented separately on the same page, and MCP is limited to "HTTP streamable" servers.
- Own job, normal executor, inline on the same worker: `push(...)` at tools.rs:481-510 then `tokio::task::spawn` of `handle_queued_job` at :544 on the same worker. The quoted comment ("pushed with the parent agent job's tag and executed inline on the same worker") is real but sits in the *AIAgent* arm at :443-444, explaining why a tag override is ignored there. `# native` TS in-process is real (`do_nativets`, `windmill-worker/src/worker.rs:4428`, dispatched at :6271-6308 via the optional `windmill-runtime-nativets` crate behind the `deno_core` feature, which `all_languages` → `ce_rpi` → `ce` enables in the published image).
- `ToolResult { call_id, function_name, result, success }` is exact: `backend/windmill-ai/src/types.rs:404`, in a `StreamingEvent` enum alongside `TokenDelta`, `ReasoningTokenDelta`, `ToolCall`, `ToolCallArguments`, `ToolExecution`.
- Isolation, verbatim: `JobIsolationLevel { Undefined=0, None=1, Unshare=2, NsjailSandboxing=3 }` at `windmill-worker/src/worker.rs:781-790`; the `DISABLE_NSJAIL` lazy_static at :371-374 does `.unwrap_or(true)`; `is_sandboxing_enabled()` at :998-1006 returns false unless `DISABLE_NSJAIL=false` or the global setting is `nsjail_sandboxing`. The `nsjail` binary is built and copied into the standard image (`Dockerfile:4-21, 317`).
- Not tier-gated. `grep` for `feature = "enterprise"` / `feature = "private"` / license checks across `backend/windmill-ai/src/` and `backend/windmill-worker/src/ai/` returns nothing. Per `LICENSE`, files under `backend/` are AGPLv3 except code behind the `enterprise` flag.

WHERE IT IS OVERSTATED:
1. TERMINAL-NODE RESTRICTION (the material one). Inside a flow, an AI agent step's stream is surfaced to the flow's consumer only if the step is the LAST step — or the `early_return` node / last step within an early-return parent. `update_flow_status_with_stream_job_inner` (`windmill-worker/src/common.rs:2026-2072`) gates the `flow_status->'stream_job'` write on `check_if_last_step(...)` (:1924-1953) or `check_if_early_return_or_last_in_early_return_parent(...)`. The API resolves the flow's stream from `(flow_status->>'stream_job')::uuid` (`windmill-api/src/jobs.rs:10615`, `get_flow_stream_delta` :10500). A mid-flow agent step still streams into its own job row, but a client watching the flow gets nothing incremental from it.
2. NOT ONE PUSH CONNECTION — DB-persisted then polled. The worker's `StreamEventProcessor` serialises each event to a newline-terminated JSON line and INSERTs it into Postgres (`windmill-worker/src/ai/stream_event_processor.rs:23-101` → `windmill-common/src/result_stream.rs:27`, table `job_result_stream_v2`). The API's SSE handler (`get_job_update_sse`, jobs.rs:10135-10206, route `/getupdate_sse/{id}` at :445) polls that table on a loop and re-emits `data: {JobUpdate}\n\n`. The client edge is one connection; the pipeline is not a passthrough.
3. POLL CADENCE, AND TIGHTENING IT IS ENTERPRISE-ONLY. The loop at jobs.rs:10329-10353 uses 100 ms for the first 10 iterations, 500 ms to 100, then 3000 ms — and only with `fast=true`, which the run-and-stream endpoint does pass (:8180-8191); without it the delay is 3000 ms throughout. A caller-supplied `poll_delay_ms` is honoured only under `#[cfg(feature = "enterprise")]`; the CE build logs `"Settable poll delay requires EE"` and keeps the default (:10342-10352). So on Community Edition token deltas arrive in ~100–500 ms batches degrading to 3 s on long runs, and sub-50 ms is a paid feature.

Two smaller corrections: the client must parse two layers, not one — the SSE `data:` JSON envelope, then split its `new_result_stream` field into newline-delimited `StreamingEvent` objects. And tool jobs are pushed with `Some(ctx.job.tag.clone())` unconditionally (tools.rs:502), so a per-tool tag cannot route a tool to a different worker group; also `"agent jobs are not supported on agent workers"` (tools.rs:527-529), so agent steps will not run on Windmill agent workers at all.

Licence footnote, not a refutation: the mechanism lives in AGPL files, but the published `ghcr.io/windmill-labs/windmill` CE image is built `features=ce` (`.github/workflows/docker-image.yml:97`), and `ce → ce_rpi → ce_core` includes `private` (`backend/Cargo.toml:201`), i.e. proprietary code that `LICENSE` forbids modifying, reselling or serving as a managed service. A purely AGPL self-build uses the `oss` feature set.

REASONING: I sparse-cloned windmill-labs/windmill at main and read the named files rather than relying on docs. Every source-level assertion in the claim checked out literally, including the `format!` path synthesis, the `ToolResult` field set, the `unwrap_or(true)` on `DISABLE_NSJAIL`, the four-variant `JobIsolationLevel`, and the "inline on the same worker" comment (though that comment is scoped to the nested-agent arm, not stated generically). I looked hard for enterprise gating in `windmill-ai` and `windmill-worker/src/ai` and found none, so the capability genuinely is Community Edition — the claim is right about that, and about the feature not requiring code in the consuming application.

It fails on the streaming sentence, and it fails on one of the exact conditions the task listed. Tracing `flow_status->'stream_job'` from the API query back to `update_flow_status_with_stream_job_inner` showed the write is gated on `check_if_last_step` (or the early-return equivalent), so only a terminal agent step's stream reaches a flow consumer. Then reading `StreamEventProcessor` showed the worker persists NDJSON to Postgres rather than pushing, and the API polls at 100 ms → 500 ms → 3 s, with a settable delay compiled out of the CE build. "All arrive over one connection" is true at the browser socket but conceals a DB round-trip and a second-scale poll floor on the free tier. That is overstatement, so refuted=true with the narrower truth recorded.

I also checked the issue tracker (17 AI-agent issues, open and closed): the failures there are provider-specific (Gemini thought_signature, Claude structured output, MCP optional-argument defaults), not "inline tools don't work". Nothing there contradicts the authoring mechanism.

Saving identical snapshot fields or changing metadata alone doesn't create a version.\" Restore \"copies its configuration into a new inactive draft\" \u2014 so rollback is testable before it goes live. No redeploy: `mastra.getAgentById('support-agent', { status: 'draft' | 'published' })` or `{ versionId: 'abc-123' }`, and over HTTP `GET /api/agents/support-agent?status=draft` / `?versionId=abc-123`. Version selection propagates to sub-agents.\n\n**The surface is documented and public**, not an internal route: `PATCH /api/stored/agents/:id` creates a draft (or publishes with `autoPublish`), `POST /stored/agents/:id/versions/:versionId/activate` publishes, plus `mastra.getEditor()` in process and the `@mastra/client-js` SDK. Full REST route set lives in `packages/server/src/server/handlers/` \u2014 `stored-agents.ts`, `agent-versions.ts`, `stored-prompt-blocks.ts`, `prompt-block-versions.ts`, `editor-builder.ts`.\n\n**Git-trackable: yes, first-class.** `new MastraEditor({ source: 'code', codePath: './mastra/editor' })` serialises \"active agent overrides ... as deterministic JSON under `<codePath>/agents/<encodedAgentId>.json`\" \u2014 one file per agent containing only Editor-managed fields (instructions plus per-tool description overrides). \"Git versions are read-only in Studio and use the commit message as their change message. The default history scan reads up to 50 recent commits.\" Studio can write the file locally, offer it as a download, or \"open a pull request instead\" with a source-control integration. That deterministic JSON is the closest thing to a DSL export; there is no separate DSL, and Editor \"doesn't generate TypeScript or change the file where the agent was created.\"\n\nPrompt blocks are the reusable unit: authored and published under **Prompts**, shared across agents, and \"When a prompt block changes, every agent that references its published version receives the update.\" That is a straight replacement for what we currently get from Langfuse prompt management, with tool selection thrown in \u2014 which Langfuse does not version.",
 "otel_export": "Native OTLP, via `@mastra/otel-exporter` (`observability/otel-exporter/`), configured with the standard `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_*` environment variables and plugged in as `new Observability({ configs: { otel: { serviceName, exporters: [new OtelExporter({ ... })] } } })`.\n\nIt emits GENERATION spans with prompts and completions, and withholds nothing by default. `packages/core/src/observability/types/tracing.ts` defines `SpanType.MODEL_GENERATION = 'model_generation'` (line 46) with `ModelGenerationInput { messages: MessageListInput; schema?: unknown }` (line 990) and `ModelGenerationResult { text?, object?, reasoning?, reasoningText?, files?, sources?, toolCalls?, warnings? }` (line 1005). The converter maps those onto current GenAI semantic conventions rather than the deprecated pair: `observability/otel-exporter/src/span-converter.test.ts:632` asserts `gen_ai.input.messages` and `gen_ai.output.messages` are set while `gen_ai.prompt`, `gen_ai.completion`, `mastra.input` and `mastra.output` are all `undefined`. `gen-ai-semantics.ts` additionally emits `gen_ai.system_instructions`, `gen_ai.tool.definitions`, `gen_ai.tool.name`, `gen_ai.request.model`/`temperature`/`top_p`/`max_tokens`/`seed`, `gen_ai.response.finish_reasons`/`id`, `gen_ai.conversation.id`, `gen_ai.agent.id`/`name`, and \u2014 usefully for our latency work \u2014 `gen_ai.usage.cache_read_input_tokens` and `gen_ai.usage.cache_creation_input_tokens`.\n\n**Could it replace Langfuse?** Functionally yes for tracing: prompts, completions, tool definitions, token and cache usage all arrive as OTLP GenAI spans, and Mastra's own Editor covers the prompt-versioning half of what Langfuse does for us today (see prompt_versioning). Two things to weigh: our Langfuse is v4 in `events_only` mode with the read API disabled, so nothing in our tooling reads Langfuse's API anyway \u2014 swapping the exporter's destination is cheap. And Mastra ships a **first-party Langfuse exporter** (`observability/langfuse/`) alongside Arize, Arthur, Braintrust, Datadog, DeepEval, Laminar, LangSmith, PostHog and Sentry, so it can equally sit beside Langfuse with zero migration. Recommended path: keep Langfuse, point Mastra's Langfuse exporter at it, and evaluate replacement separately.\n\nPayload suppression exists as `tracingPolicy` on span options (`tracing.ts:1182, 1563, 1705`); I did not read its field set, so treat granular prompt-redaction control as unverified.",
 "accepts_traceparent": "**In process: yes, verified. Over HTTP: no, not natively \u2014 you must add OTel HTTP instrumentation yourself.**\n\nIn process, `@mastra/otel-bridge` parents every Mastra span under the ambient OTel context: `observability/otel-bridge/src/bridge.ts` calls `otelContext.active()` (lines 158, 182), optionally overrides with an explicit span context (`otelTrace.setSpanContext`, lines 153, 208), then `this.otelTracer.startSpan(...)` and `otelTrace.setSpan(parentOtelContext, otelSpan)` (lines 214, 224). So if our TAC service calls `agent.stream()` inside our existing `turn.voice` span, Mastra's `AGENT_RUN` / `MODEL_GENERATION` spans land as children of it and the trace stays a single tree. This is the configuration our migration would actually use, and it is the answer that decides the question.\n\nOver HTTP, I found no inbound extraction anywhere in Mastra's own code. `grep -rn \"instrumentation-http|registerInstrumentations|propagation.extract\"` across `packages/`, `observability/` and `server-adapters/` returns **nothing**. The `traceparent` hits in the repo are all outbound or client-side: `observability/mastra/src/client/{proxy,w3c}.ts` formats and parses W3C carriers for browser-tool spans, and `packages/core/src/agent/agent.ts:1646` accepts `parentContext?: { traceparent, tracestate, baggage }` but only inside `#extractClientObservability()`, which reads a `__mastraObservability` block off a **tool-result message body** \u2014 not an HTTP header. Continuation across the HTTP boundary therefore depends on us registering `@opentelemetry/instrumentation-http` (or the framework equivalent) in the hosting process so a server span exists in `context.active()`; `observability/_examples/otel-bridge/agent-hub/README.md` is exactly that setup, a Fastify server whose tests \"Validate trace context propagation\" and \"Trace context propagation via `traceparent` header.\"\n\nBottom line for a migration: one trace, not two \u2014 provided we embed in process, or wire standard OTel HTTP instrumentation if we ever go over the network.",
 "prompt_prefix_stability": "Stable by design, and Mastra documents prompt-cache preservation as an explicit goal \u2014 this is the strongest showing of any concern in the sweep. No run id is injected into the prefix; I looked. The main path assembles system messages through `MessageList.getSystemMessages()` (`packages/core/src/agent/agent.ts:6789`) from `getInstructions()`, which returns either the static string or the result of an `({ requestContext }) => string` resolver. The only `role: 'system'` literals in `agent.ts` (lines 3779, 3810) are in thread-title generation, a side path.\n\nDocumented cache-aware behaviour, all verifiable in `docs/src/content/en/docs/`:\n- `guides/context-engineering.mdx:67` \u2014 \"If the resolved instructions change often, the model provider may not be able to reuse the same prompt cache prefix. Keep the stable part first, and pass frequently changing background through messages or signals instead.\"\n- `memory/message-history.mdx:142` \u2014 token-budget trimming removes history in chunks (`atMaxRemoveTokens`, default 25% of `maxTokens`): \"Removing history in chunks keeps the prompt prefix stable across several turns, which helps provider prompt caches stay warm.\" And \"System messages, context, the current turn's input, and the agent's responses are never trimmed.\"\n- `agents/processors.mdx:679` \u2014 transient signals are appended near the latest turn rather than modifying system messages, which \"keeps a stable prompt cache prefix across turns.\" Line 594 makes the same point for last-step reminders.\n- Observational Memory is pitched squarely at this: \"Observations are added in stable chunks, which helps providers reuse the existing prompt prefix\", with `activateAfterIdle: \"auto\"` resolving to \"a provider-aware prompt cache TTL\" and `activateOnProviderChange` forcing compression before a provider switch invalidates the cache.\n\nTwo ways to break it, both documented and both avoidable \u2014 and both are the exact trap our own memory block already walked into:\n- **The default `lastMessages: 10` sliding window.** `message-history.mdx:123`: \"The window also slides forward on every request: once a thread grows past the limit, the oldest message leaves context on each turn, which changes the start of the prompt and invalidates the provider prompt cache.\" Use `messageHistory: { maxTokens, atMaxRemoveTokens }` or Observational Memory instead.\n- **Working memory lives in the system prompt by default.** `memory/observational-memory.mdx:338`: \"Working memory normally lives in the system prompt, so updates can invalidate the prompt cache. OM-managed working memory defaults `workingMemory.useStateSignals` to `true`, which moves working memory into state signals instead.\" Set `useStateSignals: true`.\n\nNot verified: whether tool definitions are serialised in a byte-stable order run to run (see unverified).",
 "owns_history": "Mastra owns it, keyed by its own ids, and re-sends it per turn \u2014 so the two-histories-drifting failure mode is real and has to be designed against.\n\n`@mastra/core` Memory stores threads and messages against a `thread` id and a `resource` id, both supplied per call: `agent.stream(messages, { memory: { thread, resource } })`. `docs/src/content/en/docs/memory/message-history.mdx`: \"Threads and messages are created automatically when you call `agent.generate()` or `agent.stream()`\", and \"**Automatic inclusion**: Mastra automatically includes recent messages in the context window. The default of 10 messages keeps agents grounded in the conversation. Adjust it with `lastMessages`.\" So yes: history is re-read from Mastra's store and re-sent on every turn, not held in a server-side session. `lastMessages` \"counts every stored message, including tool calls, tool results, and signals of any kind, so a single turn can add several messages to the count.\"\n\nThe server layer treats those ids as security-relevant, which is a good sign: `packages/server/src/server/handlers/agents.ts` resolves an effective resource id, calls `requireEffectiveResourceId`, and runs `enforceThreadAccess` with `MEMORY_WRITE` before touching an existing thread.\n\nTwo clean resolutions for our stack, both supported. Either **make Mastra authoritative** \u2014 map the ConversationRelay call SID to `thread` and the caller number to `resource`, and stop maintaining our own turn history; or **keep our history authoritative** and omit the `memory` option entirely, passing the full message array per turn, since memory is an optional constructor argument on `Agent`. What we must not do is run both: TAC's memory Recall and Mastra's `lastMessages` window would each inject a version of the same conversation, and our own tracing already shows TAC's turn-1 Recall sitting inside `caller.turn`.",
 "embeddable_in_node": "Yes \u2014 this is Mastra's primary shape, not a supported afterthought. Install `@mastra/core`, import `{ Agent } from '@mastra/core/agent'` and `{ createTool } from '@mastra/core/tools'`, construct the agent, and call `await agent.stream(messages, opts)` in the same process. The entry point for our seam is the `textStream` getter on the returned `MastraModelOutput` (`packages/core/src/stream/base/output.ts:1825`), a `ReadableStream<string>` \u2014 async-iterable, so it feeds TAC's text-stream method with no adapter beyond `for await`.\n\nEngines: Node `>=22.13.0` (`packages/core/package.json`), satisfied by our Node 24. No build step is required to *use* the library; `mastra build` exists only for the standalone-server deployment mode we would skip.\n\nIf we later want Studio's Editor reading the same agents, we either mount Mastra's routes into an existing Express/Hono app via `server-adapters/` (`docs/src/content/en/docs/server/server-adapters`), or run a second small process against the shared store. Neither forces our TAC service to become a Mastra server. `@mastra/editor` + a store (LibSQL file or Postgres) is all the Editor needs, and `MastraCompositeStore` can route Editor data to a separate backend from application data.",
 "visual_editing": "Strong on prompts, real but bounded on tools \u2014 the best visual authoring in the sweep for the half of requirement 3 it does cover.\n\nA non-engineer can, in Studio \u2192 Agents \u2192 *agent* \u2192 **Editor**: rewrite the system prompt or layer instruction blocks on top of the code default; insert request-context values with `{{userName}}` template syntax; attach display conditions so a block shows only for a given customer, role or feature flag; compose from shared **prompt blocks** authored under **Prompts** and reused across agents (change the refund policy once, three agents get it); save as a **draft**, test it in the chat panel against the live agent, then **publish**; and restore an older version into a new draft. `docs/src/content/en/docs/studio/editor.mdx` frames it as \"a CMS for Mastra agents.\"\n\nThey can **wire** a tool: add or remove tools from a picker, rewrite a tool's description for that agent (\"A more specific description helps the agent understand when to use the tool without changing the tool itself\"), define conditional tool sets keyed on request context, and \u2014 with access \u2014 create a stored MCP client in Studio pointing at a `stdio` command or an HTTP URL and select which of its tools this agent may use. They cannot write the tool body. Developers cap the blast radius per agent with the `editor` field: `false` (nothing), `{ instructions: true }`, `{ tools: true }`, `{ tools: { description: true } }`; `id`, `name` and `model` always come from code.\n\nSurrounding surface, from `docs/src/content/en/docs/studio/overview.mdx`: run a tool standalone to debug it, follow reasoning and tool-call output live, inspect input/output processors by name and type, browse the workspace filesystem, edit request context as JSON or via a schema-driven form when the agent declares a `requestContextSchema`, attach scorers, build datasets from CSV/JSON, and run experiments comparing two versions side by side. Traces and logs are in the same UI. Studio is deployable for the team, with optional auth.\n\nSo: prompt authoring is genuinely non-engineer-ready and beats what we have. Tool authoring is selection plus description, and the implementation stays a developer task.",
 "maturity": "Substantial and moving fast. 28,105 stars, 2,797 forks, 562 open issues; repo created 2024-08-06, last push 2026-09-16 (today). GitHub's licence detector reports NOASSERTION because of the `ee/` dual-licence, not because the licence is unclear.\n\nRelease cadence is weekly on `@mastra/core`, minor-version each time: 1.67.0 (2026-09-15), 1.66.0 (09-11), 1.65.0 (09-09), 1.64.0 (09-04), 1.63.0 (08-28). Commit cadence: **100+ commits in the 7 days** to 2026-09-16 (the GitHub commits API returned a full page of 100 for `since=2026-09-09`). A ~30-package pnpm monorepo (core, server, deployer, cli, editor, playground, memory, rag, mcp, evals, plus `observability/*`, `stores/*`, `voice/*`, `auth/*`, `channels/*`, `server-adapters/*`).\n\nCorporate steward: **Kepler Software, Inc.**, per both copyright notices \u2014 a company with a paid platform (\"Mastra platform\" / Mastra Cloud) and an enterprise tier. That is the usual open-core alignment: the runtime is genuinely open and the collaboration/governance surface is where money lives.\n\n**Nobody publishes images.** There is no Dockerfile anywhere in the repo (`git ls-tree -r HEAD | grep -i dockerfile` \u2192 empty), and I could not enumerate `ghcr.io/mastra-ai` (403, token lacks `read:packages`). We would build our own, which is what our stack already does.\n\nTwo velocity hazards worth pricing in. Minor releases weekly on a 1.x line means pinning matters \u2014 the codebase itself carries compatibility shims for `@mastra/core < 1.32.0` and `< 1.34.0`, so cross-package version skew is a known real failure. And several features we might reach for are explicitly pre-stable: Code mode is `:::beta` with \"Breaking changes may occur without a major version bump until the API is stable\", `smoothStream` is `@experimental`, and `@mastra/editor` is on a `0.15.1-alpha.1` version string while `@mastra/agent-builder` is `1.1.20-alpha.1`. The Editor \u2014 the thing we would lean on hardest \u2014 is the alpha-versioned package.",
 "fit_verdict": "**Passes requirements 1 and 2 decisively. Fails requirement 3. Recommend it anyway, as the runtime, if requirement 3 is the thing that bends \u2014 and be explicit that it is requirement 3 that bends, not Mastra that stretches.**\n\nThe critic was right that this was the sweep's biggest hole, and on two of the three filters Mastra is the best answer found. Requirement 2 is not merely satisfied, it is satisfied at the exact shape of our seam: `agent.stream()` runs in process in our Node service and `stream.textStream` is a `ReadableStream<string>` of `text-delta` payloads, which is an async iterable of text \u2014 the argument TAC's voice channel already takes. No HTTP hop, no custom frame splitter, no re-assembly. Tool calls interleave inside the same stream (`tool-call-delta` is in the chunk union; `agent.test.ts:4090` drives a three-step correction loop through one `fullStream`), so a turn that calls `search_knowledge` still emits prose incrementally. Requirement 1 holds: Apache-2.0 core, a self-contained Node build or straight library use, pluggable Postgres/LibSQL storage, Studio as a static SPA, and no hosted control plane unless we set `MASTRA_LICENSE_KEY`. The enterprise carve-out is real but sits on RBAC/SSO/FGA and the Agent Builder \u2014 none of which we need, and none of which would deliver requirement 3 either.\n\nRequirement 3 fails on the docs' own words: \"Collaborators choose from the tools available to Editor, but they can't implement new tools in Studio.\" I verified that against the API rather than accepting the prose \u2014 `handlers/tools.ts` has no create route, and the EE Agent Builder's `tools: { allowed?: string[] }` is an allowlist filter over code-registered tools. Code mode executes *model*-authored code, not builder-authored code, and `@mastra/agent-builder` writes TypeScript into a repo that then needs review and restart. I am not going to stretch any of those into a pass.\n\nWhat is worth noticing is that Mastra decomposes requirement 3 into two halves and nails the harder-to-buy one. Prompts, prompt blocks, tool *selection*, per-agent tool descriptions and conditional tool sets are all authored in the UI and \u2014 the part we specifically asked about \u2014 **versioned as one snapshot**: \"Editor stores tool selections as part of an agent version\", with draft/publish, restore-into-draft, per-request `status`/`versionId` selection with no redeploy, and a `source: 'code'` mode that serialises overrides to deterministic JSON for pull-request review and Git history. That is a strict superset of what Langfuse gives us today, since Langfuse versions the prompt but not the tool set. Add native OTLP GenAI spans carrying `gen_ai.input.messages`, `gen_ai.output.messages`, `gen_ai.system_instructions`, `gen_ai.tool.definitions` and cache-token counts, and one runtime plausibly retires both the hand-written agent loop and the separate prompt service \u2014 while a first-party Langfuse exporter means we do not have to decide that on day one.\n\nThe four things round one missed all come back favourably. In-process tracing stays one tree (`OtelBridge` parents under `otelContext.active()`). The prompt prefix is stable by design, with cache preservation stated as an explicit goal across four separate docs \u2014 and the two documented ways to break it, the sliding `lastMessages` window and working memory living in the system prompt, are precisely the trap our own uncapped memory block already fell into, with `messageHistory: { maxTokens }` and `workingMemory.useStateSignals: true` as the documented fixes. History ownership is the one live design decision: Mastra keys threads by its own `thread`/`resource` ids and re-sends the window per turn, so we pick one owner \u2014 map the call SID to `thread`, or omit `memory` entirely and keep passing our own array \u2014 and must not run both alongside TAC's Recall.\n\nCost of adoption, honestly: keep orchestration as an **agent**, not a workflow (workflow streams are lifecycle events, not tokens); do not enable `BatchPartsProcessor` (batchSize 5) or `smoothStream` (10 ms coalescing) on the voice path; do not request `structuredOutput` on a turn destined for TTS; pin versions tightly, because weekly 1.x minors and in-repo `< 1.32.0` compatibility shims say cross-package skew bites; and accept that `@mastra/editor`, the piece we would lean on hardest, ships as `0.15.1-alpha.1`. The one number that would change this verdict is unmeasured: in-process time from `agent.stream()` to first `text-delta`, against our 84 ms framework baseline.",
 "disqualifiers": [
  "REQUIREMENT 3 FAILS \u2014 tool implementations cannot be authored in the builder. Verbatim, docs/src/content/en/docs/studio/editor.mdx: \"Collaborators choose from the tools available to Editor, but they can't implement new tools in Studio.\" All four sources in reference/editor/tools.mdx are pointers: Registered tools (our createTool TypeScript), MCP clients (a stdio/HTTP server we or a third party run), legacy Integration tools, Tool providers (Composio/Arcade SaaS). What the UI authors is selection plus a per-agent description override.",
  "Confirmed against the API, not just the prose: packages/server/src/server/handlers/tools.ts exposes only GET /tools, GET /tools/:toolId and POST /tools/:toolId/execute (plus per-agent equivalents). There is no create-tool route. tool-providers.ts is catalogue browse, schema read and OAuth connection management only.",
  "The enterprise tier does not buy requirement 3 either. packages/core/src/agent-builder/ee/types.ts gives admins `tools: { allowed?: string[] }` \u2014 an allowlist of tool IDs for the picker, \"Matched against the registered tools at request time\", \"Unknown IDs are dropped\". A filter over code-registered tools, not an authoring surface.",
  "Code mode does not count. createCodeMode() has the MODEL write TypeScript at inference time; \"your tools keep running on the host with full validation\". @mastra/agent-builder writes TypeScript into a repo needing review, build and restart. Neither is builder-authored tool implementation.",
  "Proprietary EE carve-out. ee/LICENSE: production use requires \"a written agreement with Kepler Software, Inc.\", where \"'production' means any use of the Software beyond development and testing on your own systems\", and \"it is forbidden to copy, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell the Software.\" Gates RBAC/SSO/FGA (core/src/auth/ee), Agent Builder (core/src/agent-builder/ee) and editor/src/ee. Enforced by a licence client POSTing to https://license.mastra.ai. None of it is a capability we need \u2014 but do not deploy the Agent Builder to a public host without a licence.",
  "One unconditional enterprise import at boot: packages/server/src/server/fga-permissions.ts:31 `import * as authEE from '@mastra/core/auth/ee';` \u2014 static, not lazy. That module is `import './telemetry'; export * from '@internal/auth/ee';`, installing a PostHog EE-telemetry bridge on globalThis as a side effect. Mild (the imported value is a permission-string map with a hardcoded fallback, everything functional is behind gated `await import`, and agent-builder-ee-static-imports.test.ts fails the suite on other static EE value imports), and moot if we embed in process and never import @mastra/server.",
  "Workflow streaming is NOT token-level. The streaming guide: workflow streaming \"returns a sequence of structured events describing the run lifecycle, rather than incremental text chunks.\" Orchestrating the voice turn as a workflow instead of an agent loses token text at the top level.",
  "Two opt-in coalescing windows that would wreck ConversationRelay if enabled: BatchPartsProcessor (batchSize default 5, optional maxWaitTime) and smoothStream (@experimental, delayInMs default 10, word/line re-chunking).",
  "The HTTP SSE payload on /agents/:agentId/stream is Mastra's own ChunkType, not chat.completion.chunk \u2014 a standard SSE client reads the framing, an OpenAI chat-completions client cannot read the semantics. (POST /v1/responses is genuinely OpenAI-Responses-compatible, emitting `event: response.output_text.delta`.)",
  "No inbound traceparent extraction anywhere in Mastra's code \u2014 grep for instrumentation-http / registerInstrumentations / propagation.extract across packages/, observability/ and server-adapters/ returns nothing. HTTP-boundary trace continuation requires us to register OTel HTTP instrumentation. In-process embedding is unaffected.",
  "Alpha/beta on the pieces we would lean on: @mastra/editor is 0.15.1-alpha.1, @mastra/agent-builder 1.1.20-alpha.1, Code mode is `:::beta` with \"Breaking changes may occur without a major version bump\", smoothStream is @experimental. Weekly 1.x minors plus in-repo compatibility shims for @mastra/core < 1.32.0 and < 1.34.0 mean version skew is a known real failure.",
  "No first-party container image \u2014 zero Dockerfiles in the repo; we build our own.",
  "CVE-2025-61685 / GHSA-xh92-rqrq-227v (medium, 2025-09-24): directory traversal \u2192 directory listing in @mastra/mcp-docs-server <= 0.13.8, patched 0.17.0. A developer docs MCP server, not the runtime; no advisories against @mastra/core, @mastra/mcp, @mastra/deployer, @mastra/server or @mastra/client-js.",
  "LocalSandbox \"runs the function as a host `node` process with host privileges\" and \"isn't isolated or secure\" \u2014 only reachable if we opt into Code mode or Workspaces, which a voice agent with HTTP tools does not need."
 ],
 "citations": [
  "https://github.com/mastra-ai/mastra \u2014 repo, sparse clone at HEAD 2026-09-16 (28,105 stars, 2,797 forks, 562 open issues, created 2024-08-06)",
  "LICENSE.md (repo root) \u2014 dual-licence declaration naming @mastra/core/auth/ee, @mastra/core/agent-builder/ee, @mastra/editor/ee; Apache-2.0 for everything else; Copyright (c) 2025 Kepler Software, Inc.",
  "ee/LICENSE \u2014 Mastra Enterprise Edition License Version 1.0, effective August 24 2026: production use requires a written agreement with Kepler; \"production\" defined as any use beyond development and testing on your own systems; copy/merge/publish/distribute/sublicense/sell forbidden; automatic termination with 30-day cure",
  "docs/src/content/en/docs/license.mdx \u2014 dual-licence doc: EE code \"require[s] a valid enterprise license for production use but can be freely used for development and testing\"",
  "packages/_internals/auth/src/ee/license.ts \u2014 LicenseClient reads MASTRA_LICENSE_KEY / MASTRA_EE_LICENSE, POSTs {MASTRA_LICENSE_URL}/validate default https://license.mastra.ai, 24h TTL, 72h grace; isDevEnvironment() line 502; isEEEnabled() line 514",
  "packages/core/src/auth/ee/index.ts + telemetry.ts \u2014 `import './telemetry'; export * from '@internal/auth/ee';`, installing a PostHog captureEEEvent bridge on globalThis",
  "packages/server/src/server/fga-permissions.ts:31 \u2014 static value namespace import `import * as authEE from '@mastra/core/auth/ee';` with FALLBACK_PERMISSIONS map",
  "packages/server/src/server/agent-builder-ee-static-imports.test.ts \u2014 regression test forbidding static value imports of @mastra/core/agent-builder/ee",
  "packages/core/src/agent-builder/ee/types.ts \u2014 BuilderModelPolicy, BuilderAgentDefaults, AgentFeatures, BUILDER_FEATURE_DEFAULTS; tools/agents/workflows allowlists \"Matched against the registered tools at request time\"",
  "packages/editor/src/index.ts \u2014 resolveBuilder() / resolveWorkflowBuilder() \u2192 assertBuilderLicensed() \u2192 `await import('@mastra/core/auth/ee')` isEEEnabled(); lazy `await import('./ee')` at lines 362, 393",
  "packages/core/src/stream/base/output.ts \u2014 `get fullStream()` line 1486; `get textStream()` line 1825 returning ReadableStream<string> from text-delta payloads; structuredOutput 'direct' array path via createJsonTextStreamTransformer",
  "packages/core/src/stream/types.ts \u2014 ChunkType union: text-delta (866), tool-call (877), tool-call-approval (878), tool-call-suspended (879), tool-call-input-streaming-start (881), tool-call-delta (882), tool-call-input-streaming-end (883)",
  "packages/core/src/agent/agent.test.ts:4090-4110 \u2014 three-step tool loop (tool-error \u2192 tool-result \u2192 text) consumed through a single output.fullStream, callCount === 3",

### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Mastra is Apache-2.0 (core) with a proprietary "Mastra Enterprise Edition License v1.0" carve-out for every `ee/` directory; GitHub reports NOASSERTION; OSI approved: true; and self-hosted commercial internal use of the capabilities we need is permitted with nothing behind a paid tier — the ee/ trees being core/auth/ee, core/agent-builder/ee and editor/src/ee, gating only Agent Builder, Workflow Builder and RBAC/SSO/FGA, with Studio, tracing, streaming, tools, memory and the plain Editor entirely on the Apache-2.0 surface.

CORRECTED: The licence text, the EE licence client, and GitHub's NOASSERTION detection are all quoted accurately — I verified every file, line number and env var cited. Three things are wrong or overstated:

1. **The `ee/` inventory is incomplete, and Studio is NOT wholly Apache-2.0.** The `main` tree has **seven** directories named `ee`, not three-plus-editor: `ee/`, `packages/_internals/auth/src/ee`, `packages/core/src/agent-builder/ee`, `packages/core/src/auth/ee`, `packages/editor/src/ee`, **`packages/playground-ui/src/ee`** and **`packages/playground/src/ee`**. The last two are the **Signals / Trace Intelligence observability UI inside Studio** (theme flows, lifelines, sankey signals, entity index, trace insight, trace-signal settings). `packages/playground-ui/src/ee/index.ts` is `export * from './topics'; export * from './signals';` and `packages/playground/src/App.tsx:14-16` statically imports `./ee/signals` and mounts it at routes (lines 396-403) — so Studio's app shell unconditionally compiles EE-licensed code, with no licence branch. The claim's own citation of `packages/_internals/auth/src/ee/license.ts` is itself a file inside an `ee/` tree it says does not exist. So "Studio ... permitted with no restriction beyond Apache-2.0's notice requirement" and "nothing on the ... tracing ... path touches a licence check" are wrong as stated: the trace-analysis surface of Studio falls inside the EE licence's scope, whose "production" definition is "any use of the Software beyond development and testing on your own systems."

2. **"OSI approved: true" is false for the artifact.** GitHub's API returns `{"key": "other", "spdx_id": "NOASSERTION"}` — the claim states this and then contradicts it. Apache-2.0 is OSI-approved; the Mastra distribution as shipped is not a single OSI-approved licence, and the EE portion is source-available, not open source. Note also `packages/agent-builder/package.json` declares `"license": "Apache-2.0"`, which npm-based tooling will read as a clean OSI result.

3. **"nothing behind a paid tier" is only true of a narrower surface.** Gated for production: Agent Builder, Workflow Builder, RBAC/SSO/FGA, and (by licence scope) Studio Signals. The defensible statement is: *the agent runtime, `agent.stream()`, tools, memory, the HTTP server, the OTel exporters and the non-`ee` Editor (instruction-builder, template-engine, rule-evaluator, workspace-tools-config, draft/publish versioning) are Apache-2.0 with no licence check.*

Verified as stated: root `LICENSE.md` wording and `Copyright (c) 2025 Kepler Software, Inc.`; `ee/LICENSE` is "Version 1.0 (Effective August 24, 2026)", `Copyright (c) 2026`, with the exact production-agreement clause, the forbidden copy/merge/publish/distribute/sublicense/sell sentence, automatic termination and the 30-day cure; `docs/src/content/en/docs/license.mdx` repeats it; `license.ts` (520 lines) reads `MASTRA_LICENSE_KEY || MASTRA_EE_LICENSE` (line 80), POSTs `${MASTRA_LICENSE_URL}/validate` defaulting to `https://license.mastra.ai` (lines 81, 180), `DEFAULT_TTL_MS = 24h` (line 74), `GRACE_PERIOD_MS = 72h` (line 73); `isDevEnvironment()` is at line 502 keyed on `MASTRA_DEV`/`NODE_ENV`, `isEEEnabled()` at line 514 returns true when dev; `packages/editor/src/index.ts:426` `assertBuilderLicensed()` throws the quoted string; `fga-permissions.ts:31` is the static `import * as authEE from '@mastra/core/auth/ee'` with a hardcoded fallback permission map; the regression test exists and covers only `@mastra/core/agent-builder/ee`, not `auth/ee`. One unmentioned detail: at line 237 the client comments "Mock a success to fail open" when the licence server is unreachable — enforcement fails open, so a self-hosted deployment can run EE features without a key, which is a licence-compliance question rather than a technical gate.

REASONING: I read the primary files directly rather than docs or marketing. The licence quotations, the dual-licence structure, GitHub's NOASSERTION, and every enforcement detail (env vars, licence URL, 24h TTL, 72h grace, both line numbers, the thrown message, the static-import shim and its regression test) check out exactly. The claim fails on scope: enumerating the repo's git tree shows seven `ee/` directories, two of them the Studio Signals/Trace-Intelligence UI, which `packages/playground/src/App.tsx` imports statically and routes to. That contradicts the claim's assertions that Studio and the tracing path are entirely Apache-2.0 and that nothing needed sits behind a paid tier. The claim also asserts "OSI approved: true" while simultaneously reporting NOASSERTION. Directionally right, overstated on scope — refuted per the standing rule.

### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Mastra allows tool definitions AND their implementations to be authored inside the builder rather than in our application source.

CORRECTED: Mastra does NOT let tool implementations be authored in the builder — the headline capability claim is false, and every quote in the supporting analysis checks out verbatim. But the analysis is itself overstated in two ways that matter for a build decision.

What is confirmed, word for word, from primary sources:
- `docs/src/content/en/docs/studio/editor.mdx`: "Tools let an agent take actions. Collaborators choose from the tools available to Editor, but they can't implement new tools in Studio." and "**Project tools** must be implemented and registered in the Mastra project by a developer."
- `docs/src/content/en/reference/editor/tools.mdx`: four sources (Registered tools, MCP clients, Integration tools, Tool providers), all pointers; "The description changes what the model sees without modifying the original tool definition"; conditional variants keyed on request context; a five-step resolution order.
- `packages/server/src/server/handlers/tools.ts` defines exactly five routes (`LIST_TOOLS_ROUTE`, `GET_TOOL_BY_ID_ROUTE`, `EXECUTE_TOOL_ROUTE`, `GET_AGENT_TOOL_ROUTE`, `EXECUTE_AGENT_TOOL_ROUTE`) — no create route. `tool-providers.ts` is 13 routes of catalogue browse, schema read, and OAuth/connection management only.
- `packages/core/src/agent-builder/ee/types.ts` `tools: { allowed?: string[] }` is an allowlist: "omitted (`undefined`) ⇒ unrestricted", "`allowed: []` ⇒ empty picker", "Matched against the registered tools at request time. Unknown IDs are dropped and surfaced as warnings."
- Code mode is real and as described: `@mastra/core@1.38.0`, marked beta ("Breaking changes may occur without a major version bump"), "your tools keep running on the host with full validation."
- `@mastra/agent-builder` is Apache-2.0, version `1.1.20-alpha.1`.

Correction 1 — the licence conclusion is wrong for the actual Agent Builder. The analysis examined `packages/core/src/agent-builder/ee/types.ts` and reported the Apache-2.0 `@mastra/agent-builder` package, but the browser Agent Builder is a different thing: `docs/.../reference/editor/mastra-editor.mdx` says "The Builder UI runs against an agent created by the `createBuilderAgent()` factory from `@mastra/editor/ee`", implemented in `packages/editor/src/ee/agent-builder-agent.ts`. Root `LICENSE.md` states that "All content that resides under any directory named `ee/`… including… `@mastra/core/agent-builder/ee`, `@mastra/editor/ee`" is under `ee/LICENSE` — the Mastra Enterprise Edition License v1.0, which says the software "may only be used in production if you… have entered into… a written agreement with Kepler Software, Inc." and defines production as "any use of the Software beyond development and testing on your own systems." GitHub reports the repo licence as NOASSERTION/Other. So the builder surface is enterprise-licensed, not Apache. Separately, `@mastra/editor` is `0.15.1-alpha.1` with no `license` field in its `package.json` — the whole Editor feature is alpha.

Correction 2 — "no isolation boundary is introduced, because no builder-authored code exists" is too strong. Three builder-authored artifacts exist beyond description overrides and tool selection: (a) `editor.mcp` and `editor.mcpServer` are full CRUD namespaces (`create`/`update`/`delete`/`list`), and `reference/editor/tools.mdx` says "Stored clients can start a local `stdio` server", storing a `command` with optional `args`, `env`, `timeout` — a collaborator creating an MCP client in Studio therefore persists configuration that makes the host spawn a local process, which is builder-authored execution, not merely a pointer to "a server we or a third party run"; (b) the Agent Builder injects a `createSkillTool` client tool (gated by `features.skills`) that persists user-attachable stored skills — instructions, not implementations, but authored in the UI and beyond selection; (c) `MastraEditor` itself exposes `sandboxes`, `filesystems`, `blobStores`, `browsers`, `codePath`, and `source` namespaces, and code mode does introduce a sandbox boundary for model-written orchestration code.

The narrower truth: instructions, prompt blocks, tool *selection*, per-agent tool descriptions, conditional tool sets, and MCP client/server configuration are authored in the UI and versioned; tool bodies stay in our TypeScript, and there is no API to create one. Executor for tool logic is our Node process (or an MCP server, which a Studio collaborator can now cause our host to spawn). The UI that does the most authoring — Agent Builder — is EE-licensed and needs a Kepler agreement for production, and Editor is at `0.15.1-alpha.1`.

Limit on this check: `agent-builder.mastra.ai`, the hosted docs the repo links to for Agent Builder reference pages, is blocked by a corporate proxy here (Zscaler CATEGORY_DENIED), so I read the in-repo EE source and docs instead of those pages.

REASONING: Every quotation in the mechanism is accurate — I fetched each cited file from `mastra-ai/mastra@main` and matched the strings, and confirmed the absence of a create-tool route by listing the route exports in `packages/server/src/server/handlers/tools.ts`. So the headline capability claim ("implementations authored inside the builder") is false, which by itself sets refuted=true. The analysis is nonetheless overstated on two counts I could prove from primary sources. First, it drew the licence conclusion from the wrong package: the browser Agent Builder is `createBuilderAgent()` from `@mastra/editor/ee`, and root `LICENSE.md` places every `ee/` directory — naming `@mastra/editor/ee` and `@mastra/core/agent-builder/ee` explicitly — under the Mastra EE License, which bars production use without a written Kepler agreement. Citing `@mastra/agent-builder`'s Apache-2.0 leaves the wrong impression about the builder surface. Second, "no builder-authored code exists" fails against `editor.mcp` CRUD plus the documented `stdio` transport storing a `command`/`args`/`env` that the host starts, against `createSkillTool` in the EE builder agent, and against the `sandboxes`/`filesystems`/`codePath` namespaces on `MastraEditor`. Also worth flagging for planning: `@mastra/editor` is `0.15.1-alpha.1` and code mode is explicitly beta, so this is pre-1.0 surface in both places.

### verdict refuted=True
CLAIM: Mastra streams token-level model output incrementally during an agent turn that includes tool calls, via three surfaces (in-process `MastraModelOutput.textStream`/`fullStream`, HTTP SSE at `POST /agents/:agentId/stream`, and OpenAI-Responses-compatible SSE at `POST /v1/responses`), with "nothing buffering by default on the agent path" apart from four opt-in features and the workflow-vs-agent architectural choice.

CORRECTED: The streaming mechanism is real, in-process, and Apache-2.0 — every named symbol exists and the implementation matches the description almost verbatim. Two things are overstated.

**Confirmed as stated.** `packages/core/src/stream/base/output.ts` `get textStream()` is exactly `this.#createEventedStream().pipeThrough(new TransformStream({ transform(chunk, controller) { if (chunk.type === 'text-delta') controller.enqueue(chunk.payload.text) } }))`, preceded only by the `#structuredOutputMode === 'direct'` + array-schema branch that routes through `createJsonTextStreamTransformer` (which the claim already flags as caveat 4). `get fullStream()` returns `__getUnfilteredFullStream()` optionally filtered by `hideSignals`. `agent.stream()` is typed `Promise<MastraModelOutput<OUTPUT>>` (`stream-until-idle.ts`, `subagent.ts`). `STREAM_GENERATE_ROUTE` in `packages/server/src/server/handlers/agents.ts` is `POST /agents/:agentId/stream`, `responseType: 'stream'`, `streamFormat: 'sse'`, and returns `streamResult.fullStream`. `server-adapters/express/src/index.ts` writes `res.write(\`data: ${serialized.json}\n\n\`)` for `streamFormat === 'sse'` and `serialized.json + '\x1E'` otherwise, sets `text/event-stream` / `Cache-Control: no-cache` / `Connection: keep-alive` / `X-Accel-Buffering: no`, and redacts by default via `this.streamOptions?.redact ?? true` → `redactStreamChunk`. `responses.adapter.ts` emits named events including `response.output_text.delta`, and `responses.test.ts` asserts `response.function_call_arguments.done` → `response.output_text.delta` → `response.completed` in one stream. `BatchPartsProcessor` defaults `batchSize: 5` (and, unmentioned, `emitOnNonText: true`, so a non-text part flushes the batch). `smoothStream` is `@experimental` with `delayInMs = 10`, `chunking = 'word'`. The docs quotes are verbatim, including the workflow-streaming caveat. `agent.test.ts` does drive tool-error → tool-result → text through one `output.fullStream` with `expect(callCount).toBe(3); // 1 wrong + 1 correct + 1 text response`, and `packages/core/src/agent/__tests__/batch-parts-downstream.test.ts` exists. License: root `LICENSE.md` puts everything outside `ee/` under Apache-2.0, and `@mastra/core` declares `"license": "Apache-2.0"`; none of the cited paths are under `ee/`.

**Overstatement 1 — "nothing buffers by default on the agent path" is false for tool events.** Text deltas do stream immediately, but two default behaviours in the agentic loop buffer, and both are open bugs with maintainer assignees. mastra-ai/mastra#21902 (open, assigned, reproduced on `@mastra/core` 1.57.0 and 1.60.0): because the inner workflow is `.foreach(toolCallStep, toolCallForeachOptions).then(llmMappingStep)` and `llmMappingStep` is what enqueues tool-result chunks, `tool-output-available` for *every* tool call in a step is held until the *last* execution finishes — measured 3.2 s of hold, and "5 image generation tool calls of about 1 minute each" arriving all at once. mastra-ai/mastra#22919 (open, assigned, 1.59.0): `tool.execute` and `onInputAvailable` cannot run until `llm-execution` has consumed `finish`, so although `tool-call`/`tool-call-delta` chunks stream as they arrive, nothing acts on complete args until the whole step's stream ends (reported ~5–20 s per multi-tool step). Also relevant if the design nests agents: #15013 (open) reports no progressive streaming through 3-level sub-agent delegation. So the accurate framing is: *text* is unbuffered by default; *tool-result emission and tool execution start are barriered by default*, which matters for a voice turn where a tool result would otherwise be spoken as soon as it lands.

**Overstatement 2 — the line numbers are unverifiable and the "sibling routes" note is wrong.** `main` is `@mastra/core@1.68.0-alpha.2` and moves daily; none of the cited anchors (output.ts 1825/1486, agents.ts ~1797, express 193, responses.ts 914, adapter 703/752/1210, types.ts 866/877/881–883, agent.test.ts 4090–4110) could be confirmed, and they should be treated as symbol references only. `/agents/:agentId/stream/ui` exists but is `STREAM_UI_MESSAGE_DEPRECATED_ROUTE` with `responseType: 'stream'` and *no* `streamFormat`, i.e. not SSE; I found no `/stream/vnext/ui`, only the likewise-deprecated `STREAM_GENERATE_VNEXT_DEPRECATED_ROUTE` at `/agents/:agentId/stream/vnext`.

**One framing note.** The claim was filed under "the Mastra agent builder." The agent builder proper is `@mastra/core/agent-builder/ee`, which `LICENSE.md` explicitly carves out of Apache-2.0 into `ee/LICENSE`. Everything asserted here is about the Apache-2.0 `Agent` class, not that enterprise surface.

REASONING: I read the actual source rather than docs. Every symbol, default value, header, and frame format the claim asserts checked out — this is the best-sourced claim of the set, and the `textStream` getter is quoted correctly down to the punctuation. Two things fail. First, the summary sentence "Nothing buffers by default on the agent path, but four opt-in things do" does not survive the issue tracker: two open, assigned, version-pinned bugs (#21902, #22919) document default buffering in the agent loop — tool-result chunks held behind a `.foreach()` barrier until the last execution in the step completes, and tool `execute` gated on the LLM step finishing its stream. For the stated use (ConversationRelay voice, where a tool result should reach TTS the moment it exists) that is exactly the class of buffering the caveat section claimed did not exist by default. Second, the claim leans on precise line numbers on a fast-moving `main` (1.68.0-alpha.2) that cannot be reproduced, and its "sibling routes" aside describes `/agents/:agentId/stream/ui` as a live AI-SDK-UI surface when the constant is literally named `STREAM_UI_MESSAGE_DEPRECATED_ROUTE` and carries no `streamFormat`. Per the instruction that overstated counts as refuted, I set refuted=true, but the narrower truth is close to the original: token-level text streaming through an agent loop with tools is real, in-process, Apache-2.0, and does not require an HTTP hop.
